# PowerPoint (PPTX): feature catalog

Level 2 of the feature catalog. Curated by hand in [pptx.yaml](pptx.yaml); this page is generated by
`dotnet run --project tools/SpecTools -- features`. Element names link to the format catalog (level 1).

Rendering target: one HTML document per presentation with fixed-size, absolutely positioned slides. Shapes with their geometry, fills, lines and effects become SVG; text is measured and broken into lines by our own layout engine and emitted as positioned glyph runs; slide shows (transitions, animations, media) run in a small JS player.

## Summary

- 146 features in 19 areas
- Coverage: 1,653 of the 1,653 elements that can occur in a PPTX file (100.0 %) are assigned to a feature

Priority: **P0** needed for typical documents, **P1** common, **P2** occasional, **P3** rare or editor-only.
Difficulty: **1** direct mapping to HTML/CSS/SVG … **5** research project.

<a id="package.opc"></a>

### Package, content types and relationships

`package.opc` · P0 · difficulty 1 · done

**Rendering:** Open XML SDK on System.IO.Packaging. Parts are found through relationships, never by path; tolerate unusual part names, missing optional parts and external (linked) targets.

**Office behaviour:** Verified: every corpus deck opens through its relationships, the password-protected ones too (generated/encrypted-agile and encrypted-standard 98.3 %).

**Elements (5):** [`ct:Default`](../format/namespaces/ct.md#ctdefault), [`ct:Override`](../format/namespaces/ct.md#ctoverride), [`ct:Types`](../format/namespaces/ct.md#cttypes), [`pr:Relationship`](../format/namespaces/pr.md#prrelationship), [`pr:Relationships`](../format/namespaces/pr.md#prrelationships)

<a id="package.mce"></a>

### Markup compatibility and extension lists

`package.mce` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

mc:AlternateContent, mc:Ignorable and the extLst/ext mechanism every newer feature uses.

**Rendering:** Take the first mc:Choice whose Requires prefixes we implement, else mc:Fallback; honour mc:Ignorable. Dispatch a:ext and p:ext on their uri (a GUID) to the handler of the extension; skip unknown ones.

**Office behaviour:** PowerPoint writes newer features twice: the new markup in mc:Choice (e.g. Requires="p14") and an older equivalent in mc:Fallback, e.g. p14 transitions fall back to p:fade, 3D models and zoom objects to a picture. Verified with the p14 transitions, a14 equations, cx charts and zoom objects of the corpus (generated/slideshow, charts-more, presentation).

**Elements (34):** [`a14:extLst`](../format/namespaces/a14.md#a14extlst), [`a3danim:extLst`](../format/namespaces/a3danim.md#a3danimextlst), [`a:ext`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aext), [`a:extLst`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aextlst), [`aanim:extLst`](../format/namespaces/aanim.md#aanimextlst), [`alf:extLst`](../format/namespaces/alf.md#alfextlst), [`am3d:extLst`](../format/namespaces/am3d.md#am3dextlst), [`ask:extLst`](../format/namespaces/ask.md#askextlst), [`asl:extLst`](../format/namespaces/asl.md#aslextlst), [`c:ext`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cext), [`c:extLst`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cextlst), [`cdr14:extLst`](../format/namespaces/cdr14.md#cdr14extlst), [`cs:extLst`](../format/namespaces/cs.md#csextlst), [`cx:ext`](../format/namespaces/cx.md#cxext), [`cx:extLst`](../format/namespaces/cx.md#cxextlst), [`dgm:extLst`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmextlst), [`dsp:extLst`](../format/namespaces/dsp.md#dspextlst), [`oac:extLst`](../format/namespaces/oac.md#oacextlst), [`p14:extLst`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14extlst), [`p1510:extLst`](../format/namespaces/p1510.md#p1510extlst), [`p15:extLst`](../format/namespaces/p15.md#p15extlst), [`p188:extLst`](../format/namespaces/p188.md#p188extlst), [`p202:extLst`](../format/namespaces/p202.md#p202extlst), [`p223:extLst`](../format/namespaces/p223.md#p223extlst), [`p228:extLst`](../format/namespaces/p228.md#p228extlst), [`p:ext`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pext), [`p:extLst`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pextlst), [`pc226:extLst`](../format/namespaces/pc226.md#pc226extlst), [`pc:extLst`](../format/namespaces/pc.md#pcextlst), [`pic14:extLst`](../format/namespaces/pic14.md#pic14extlst), [`psez:extLst`](../format/namespaces/psez.md#psezextlst), [`pslz:extLst`](../format/namespaces/pslz.md#pslzextlst), [`psuz:extLst`](../format/namespaces/psuz.md#psuzextlst), [`thm15:extLst`](../format/namespaces/thm15.md#thm15extlst)

<a id="package.encryption"></a>

### Encrypted and write-protected files

`package.encryption` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Password-protected files are OLE compound files with an EncryptedPackage stream ([MS-OFFCRYPTO], agile encryption): ask for the password and decrypt before parsing. Write protection only matters to an editor.

**Office behaviour:** A password-protected presentation is a compound file ([MS-CFB]) with an EncryptedPackage stream that EncryptionInfo describes ([MS-OFFCRYPTO]). Agile encryption (version 4.4: spun SHA-1 to SHA-512 password hash, a package key sealed with it, AES-CBC in 4096-byte segments) and standard encryption (version x.2: AES-128/192/256 keyed by 50 000 SHA-1 spins, ECB) are decrypted with the password given to the converter; without one, Office's default password VelvetSweatshop is tried, and PasswordException tells a missing password from a wrong one. Write protection (p:modifyVerifier) and the read-only recommendation (p1710:readonlyRecommended) do not change how the presentation shows and only matter to an editor. Checked with generated/encrypted-agile (encrypted by PowerPoint) and generated/encrypted-standard (written by the fidelity tool, opened by PowerPoint).

**Elements (2):** [`p1710:readonlyRecommended`](../format/namespaces/p1710.md#p1710readonlyrecommended), [`p:modifyVerifier`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pmodifyverifier)

**Specification:** §19.2.1.19 Modification Verifier (p.2545); MS-PPTX §2.14.1.1 readonlyRecommended (p.108)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1104

<a id="presentation.slides"></a>

### Slide list and slide size

`presentation.slides` · P0 · difficulty 1 · done

**Rendering:** Slides in p:sldIdLst order. Sizes are in EMU (914400 per inch) and are rendered at 96 dpi, so the widescreen default 12192000 × 6858000 EMU becomes 1280 × 720 px; the viewer scales the slides to fit.

<a id="presentation.masters"></a>

### Slide masters, layouts and slides

`presentation.masters` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Draw the master's and the layout's non-placeholder shapes behind the slide's own shapes unless showMasterSp is off on the layout or slide; then the slide's shapes in document order.

**Office behaviour:** Placeholders on layouts and masters are prompts for the editor and are never drawn on slides; only the slide's own placeholders are. Verified with generated/presentation (98.0 %); collapse, matchingName, preserve, userDrawn and showMasterPhAnim are editing state.

**Elements (7):** [`p:cSld`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pcsld), [`p:sld`](../format/namespaces/p.md#psld), [`p:sldLayout`](../format/namespaces/p.md#psldlayout), [`p:sldLayoutId`](../format/namespaces/p.md#psldlayoutid), [`p:sldLayoutIdLst`](../format/namespaces/p.md#psldlayoutidlst), [`p:sldMaster`](../format/namespaces/p.md#psldmaster), [`p:spTree`](../format/namespaces/p.md#psptree)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1121, 2.1.1128

<a id="presentation.placeholders"></a>

### Placeholders and inheritance

`presentation.placeholders` · P0 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Match a slide placeholder to the layout placeholder by idx alone (0 when missing), a layout placeholder to the master placeholder by type. Position, geometry, fill, line, body properties and list styles are inherited along that chain, property by property; the master's text style follows the type of the layout placeholder matched.

**Office behaviour:** Measured with the object model on a Title and Content layout (placeholders of 24 type and idx combinations without positions of their own): slide placeholders match layout placeholders by idx alone, 0 when it is missing, whatever their types. p:ph without idx and with type body, dt, ftr, sldNum, pic or subTitle takes the title's place and title style; type title with idx 1 takes the content placeholder's place, size and bullets. A placeholder whose idx the layout lacks inherits from no placeholder at all, not even the master's of its type: it has no position, and only the master's text style of its own type applies (dt and sldNum at 18 pt, black and left-aligned instead of the master placeholders' 12 pt gray). The idx 4294967295 (-1) stands for none: such placeholders match by type (sldNum, ftr and dt take their layout placeholders' places at 12 pt gray, body the object placeholder's), and without a layout placeholder of their type the master's (on a blank layout, title and body take the master's places, while a title without idx or with idx 20 takes none). Among several layout placeholders of the same idx the slide placeholder takes the first in the layout's tree, whatever the types (LibreOffice writes titles and subtitles without idx: its tdf160487's subtitle takes the layout title's place and title style, and the subtitle's where the layout lists the subtitle first). Verified with generated/presentation and text-basic; hasCustomPrompt, isPhoto and userDrawn are editing state.

**Elements (2):** [`p:nvPr`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pnvpr), [`p:ph`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pph)

**Specification:** §19.3.1.33 Non-Visual Properties (p.2593); §19.3.1.36 Placeholder Shape (p.2594)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1126, 2.1.1127

<a id="presentation.colorMap"></a>

### Color mapping

`presentation.colorMap` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** bg1/tx1/bg2/tx2 map to dk1/lt1/dk2/lt2 through the master's clrMap or a layout or slide override.

**Office behaviour:** The master's p:clrMap maps all twelve names (accents and hyperlink colors too), a layout's or slide's overrideClrMapping replaces it; a chart keeps its own c:clrMapOvr against the slide's mapping, for its text as well (measured with generated/colormap: dark mapping with swapped accents).

**Elements (4):** [`a:masterClrMapping`](../format/namespaces/a.md#amasterclrmapping), [`a:overrideClrMapping`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aoverrideclrmapping), [`p:clrMap`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pclrmap), [`p:clrMapOvr`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pclrmapovr)

<a id="presentation.background"></a>

### Slide background

`presentation.background` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** The slide's background, else the layout's, else the master's. bgRef idx 1001 and above index the theme's bgFillStyleLst, lower values its fillStyleLst; the reference color replaces phClr.

**Office behaviour:** Verified with generated/fills, colormap and presentation; bwMode, shadeToTitle and a15:backgroundPr only matter for black and white or legacy printing. Without a background on slide, layout and master the slide shows the color bg1 maps to (LibreOffice's layout-clrmap-override: a layout mapping bg1 to dk1 gives its slides a black background). A bgRef's picture fill names a picture of the theme part (Apache POI's themes deck).

**Elements (4):** [`a15:backgroundPr`](../format/namespaces/a15.md#a15backgroundpr), [`p:bg`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pbg), [`p:bgPr`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pbgpr), [`p:bgRef`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pbgref)

<a id="theme.styleRefs"></a>

### Shape style references

`theme.styleRefs` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Resolve idx into the theme lists; the reference's color replaces phClr in the referenced style. fontRef supplies the default text color and theme font of the shape.

**Office behaviour:** Shapes inserted in PowerPoint usually carry no direct fill or line but a p:style, so without style resolution they come out invisible or black. Verified with generated/geometry-presets, fills and outlines (inserted shapes carry only p:style). Fills and lines resolve level by level from the shape up through its layout and master placeholders, each level's own spPr before its own p:style: a slide title's style fill and line beat the master title's explicit noFill (Apache POI's customGeo deck).

**Elements (7):** [`a:effectRef`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aeffectref), [`a:fillRef`](../format/namespaces/a.md#afillref), [`a:fontRef`](../format/namespaces/a.md#afontref), [`a:lnRef`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alnref), [`a:style`](../format/namespaces/a.md#astyle), [`p:style`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pstyle), [`pic14:style`](../format/namespaces/pic14.md#pic14style)

**Specification:** §19.3.1.46 Shape Style (p.2600); §20.1.2.2.37 Shape Style (p.2763); §20.1.4.1.17 Font Reference (p.2804); §20.1.4.2.8 Effect Reference (p.2817); §20.1.4.2.10 Fill Reference (p.2818); §20.1.4.2.19 Line Reference (p.2824) and 1 more

`color.models` · P0 · difficulty 1 · done

**Rendering:** Resolve to sRGB plus alpha. sysClr uses its lastClr, prstClr the named colors, scrgbClr is linear RGB.

<a id="color.transforms"></a>

### Color transforms

`color.transforms` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Apply in document order. lumMod/lumOff work in HSL, tint and shade most likely in linear RGB; every transform is to be checked against PowerPoint renderings.

**Office behaviour:** Verified with generated/fills, colormap and smartart (theme color variations). gamma applies the sRGB gamma shift (encodes) and invGamma its inverse, so the gamma, shade, invGamma that converted PowerPoint 97 decks write shades in sRGB: 0066CC at 46.275 % is 002F5E (Apache POI's 45541 decks).

<a id="shape.basic"></a>

### Shapes

`shape.basic` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Each shape is an absolutely positioned element with an SVG for geometry, fill, line and effects plus a text layer.

**Office behaviour:** Axis-aligned rectangles and lines are drawn without antialiasing: every edge goes to the nearest pixel (fills and outlines at fractional positions), which crispEdges reproduces. p:sp/@useBgFill fills the shape with the slide's background, aligned to the slide (generated/colormap). bwMode only matters for grayscale printing. Verified across the corpus.

**Elements (8):** [`a:cNvSpPr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#acnvsppr), [`a:nvSpPr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#anvsppr), [`a:sp`](../format/namespaces/a.md#asp), [`a:spPr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#asppr), [`p:cNvSpPr`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pcnvsppr), [`p:nvSpPr`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pnvsppr), [`p:sp`](../format/namespaces/p.md#psp), [`p:spPr`](../format/namespaces/p.md#psppr)

<a id="shape.group"></a>

### Groups

`shape.group` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Map child coordinates (chOff/chExt) to the group box and compose the rotations and flips of all ancestors into one matrix per leaf shape.

**Office behaviour:** A child turned by 45 to 135 degrees (or 225 to 315) lies across a squeezed group: its width scales with the group's height and its height with the width, about its center (LibreOffice's tdf141463_GroupTransform: a child turned by 80 degrees in a group squeezed to two thirds of its height). A group's own effects (its p:grpSpPr's a:effectLst) PowerPoint does not draw (external/poi/60810 slide 22: a group's 18 pt glow and its shadow; drawn, 90.0 → 89.5 %), where Word draws them on the whole group (docx drawing.groups). A locked canvas (lc:lockedCanvas in a graphic frame) is drawn like a picture of its DrawingML shapes: the box around them stretched over the frame together with their lines and text, shapes in groups counting by their own boxes mapped through their groups (docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlimport/ fdo43641: a group reaching past its rectangle and connector, 5.4 → 91.9 %), a turned shape by its geometry as turned, flipped first (measured in Word with docx/local/lcv-*, lcw-*: two shapes stretched over frames of other sizes and shapes, offset, in a stretching group; a rectangle turned by 30, 60, 90 and 308 degrees by its turned corners, an ellipse turned by 45 by its curve, a text box's text not counted; tdf143476_lockedCanvas_position: a text box turned by 308 degrees, 74.4 → 93.7 %), its own group transform unused, text without a font in Arial (generated/objects-more). a14:isCanvas and a15:nonVisualGroupProps only mark groups for editing. Verified with generated/transforms (99.3 %) and objects-more.

<a id="shape.presetGeometry"></a>

### Preset geometries

`shape.presetGeometry` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

187 preset shapes (rect, roundRect, arrows, callouts, flowchart symbols, stars …).

**Rendering:** One formula engine evaluates presetShapeDefinitions.xml (adjust values, guides, paths) into SVG paths, so every preset comes from data instead of hand-written code.

**Office behaviour:** The ECMA data file has no upArrow and lists upDownArrow twice (see the format catalog); upArrow has to be added and checked against PowerPoint. The pie's text rectangle is il, it, ir, ib in PowerPoint, where the data file swaps its top and right (LibreOffice's tdf149918_pie_textarea). All 187 presets verified with generated/geometry-presets (99.3 %), upArrow from presetShapeSupplement.xml.

**Elements (3):** [`a:avLst`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aavlst), [`a:gd`](../format/namespaces/a.md#agd), [`a:prstGeom`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aprstgeom)

<a id="shape.customGeometry"></a>

### Custom geometry (freeforms)

`shape.customGeometry` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** The same formula engine. arcTo angles have to be converted to SVG arcs; path fill modes darken/lighten shade the fill; paths with their own w/h are scaled to the shape. Handles and connection sites are editor-only.

**Office behaviour:** PowerPoint fills all paths of a geometry before it strokes any of them (measured with overlapping paths: the outline of the first path lies on the fill of the second, also for an outline-only path saved before a fill-only one); the document does the same where the order matters (presets chartPlus, chartStar, chartX, sketched shapes). In paths with their own w/h, arcTo angles are visual angles in the path's coordinates, before it is stretched to the shape (measured: clouds of other proportions than the 43200-unit square). Subpaths of a path fill alternately, a hole where two overlap (LibreOffice's custgeom-overlapping-contours); an arcTo sweep goes round at most once (tdf122323: 400 degrees end where they start). Verified with generated/geometry-presets and sketched; adjust handles (a:ahXY, a:ahPolar) and connection sites are editor-only.

<a id="shape.sketched"></a>

### Sketched outlines

`shape.sketched` · P2 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** The hand-drawn outline styles (curved, freehand, scribble) perturb the geometry with a seeded random function; matching PowerPoint needs its exact algorithm, a close look-alike is easy.

**Office behaviour:** PowerPoint saves a sketched shape as the hand-drawn paths it derives from the original geometry and the seed: a custom geometry with an outline-only path (fill=none) followed by a fill-only path (stroke=0), and keeps the original geometry, the style and the seed (sd) in ask:lineSketchStyleProps in the outline's extension list. Opening a file it draws the sketch anew from those, which gives the saved paths again (checked through PowerPoint's own saved and re-exported decks), so the document draws the saved paths, fills before outlines, and lays out the text in the text rectangle of the original geometry (triangles and ellipses keep their inset text). A sketch on a preset geometry is dropped by PowerPoint, which then draws the sketch's original geometry (nothing if it has none); a sketch of the style none or without an original geometry leaves the custom geometry. Paths of a custom geometry that a third party saved with sketch properties but other than PowerPoint's are drawn as saved, while PowerPoint replaces them with its own (unpublished, seeded) sketch. Checked with generated/sketched (curved, freehand and scribble on five presets with text, without fill, with gradient fill, dashes, shadow, rotation and flips).

**Elements (8):** [`ask:extLst`](../format/namespaces/ask.md#askextlst), [`ask:lineSketchCurved`](../format/namespaces/ask.md#asklinesketchcurved), [`ask:lineSketchFreehand`](../format/namespaces/ask.md#asklinesketchfreehand), [`ask:lineSketchNone`](../format/namespaces/ask.md#asklinesketchnone), [`ask:lineSketchScribble`](../format/namespaces/ask.md#asklinesketchscribble), [`ask:lineSketchStyleProps`](../format/namespaces/ask.md#asklinesketchstyleprops), [`ask:seed`](../format/namespaces/ask.md#askseed), [`ask:type`](../format/namespaces/ask.md#asktype)

**Specification:** MS-ODRAWXML §2.38.1.1 lineSketchStyleProps (p.320); MS-ODRAWXML §2.38.3.1 CT_Empty (p.320); MS-ODRAWXML §2.38.3.3 CT_LineSketchTypeProperties (p.322)

<a id="fill.gradient"></a>

### Gradient fills

`fill.gradient` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** SVG linear and radial gradients in the bounds of the geometry's filled paths (lin angle with scaled, rotWithShape); circle paths map to radial gradients, rect paths to four trapezoids, path="shape" to bands of the outline. Browsers interpolate in sRGB, so the engine adds intermediate stops computed in linear light.

**Office behaviour:** Measured with gradient strips and PowerPoint's own EMF export: a two-color gradient (stops at 0 and 1) and a symmetric three-color gradient (A, B, A) use a GDI+ sigma bell blend (normal distribution CDF, sigma 0.25, normalized to 0 to 1) mixed in linear light; all other gradients interpolate linearly in sRGB. Rectangular path gradients are concentric rectangles from fillToRect to tileRect. Path gradients that follow the shape (path=shape) repeat the shape's filled outline in bands scaled from the tile rectangle to the focus rectangle, mixed in sRGB (measured with local/pathgrad: concentric ellipses, stars, triangles, hearts, parallelograms). 64 presets (block arrows, callouts with tails, ribbons, scrolls, waves, donut, moon, smiley face, sun …) draw such a gradient exactly as a circle gradient (measured: all 187 presets filled both ways; the binary format's fFillShadeShapeOK flag); custom geometries follow their outline. Every gradient spans the bounds of the geometry's filled paths rather than the shape's box (a block arc's band shows the whole gradient), and darkened or lightened paths shade the gradient's colors like a solid fill's (the faces of cans, cubes and bevels; local/pathgrad3: 99.2 %, local/pathgrad_all: 98.6 %). A gradFill without gsLst takes the stops of the style's gradient (fillRef) in its own direction (Apache POI bug 63200). A gradient that does not turn with its shape (rotWithShape 0) keeps the slide's directions through its groups' turns and flips too (openxml-sdk's o09_Performance_typical). A slide background shaded to the title (p:bgPr shadeToTitle) has its path gradient's focus on the title placeholder's box, as the slide, its layout or its master places it (LibreOffice's sd/tdf114848 slide 5: white under the master's title, D1C39F at the slide's edges; the deck 86.9 → 90.4 %). A rectangular path gradient with translucent stops lets through what lies under it, also between its sides (LibreOffice's oox/tdf139618_ImportWordArtGradient: WordArt from transparent accent1 at a corner to opaque, 87.1 → 98.8 %).

**Elements (7):** [`a:fillToRect`](../format/namespaces/a.md#afilltorect), [`a:gradFill`](../format/namespaces/a.md#agradfill), [`a:gs`](../format/namespaces/a.md#ags), [`a:gsLst`](../format/namespaces/a.md#agslst), [`a:lin`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alin), [`a:path`](../format/namespaces/a.md#apath), [`a:tileRect`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atilerect)

**Specification:** §20.1.8.31 Fill To Rectangle (p.2876); §20.1.8.33 Gradient Fill (p.2878); §20.1.8.36 Gradient stops (p.2880); §20.1.8.37 Gradient Stop List (p.2880); §20.1.8.41 Linear Gradient Fill (p.2882); §20.1.8.46 Path Gradient (p.2885) and 2 more

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1296, 2.1.1297, 2.1.1308, 2.1.1312

**Office behaviour:** The tiles lie on the slide's pixels whatever the shape's turn, also in an outline (LibreOffice's tdf107608).

<a id="fill.picture"></a>

### Picture and texture fills

`fill.picture` · P1 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** SVG image clipped to the geometry: stretched into the fillRect insets, or repeated through an SVG pattern for tiles (offset, scale, alignment, flip).

**Office behaviour:** Tiles are sized from the picture's own resolution; PowerPoint ignores blipFill dpi (measured). Verified with generated/pictures and fills; a:blip cstate (compression) does not change the picture. A shape's blipFill without stretch or tile is tiled at the picture's own size (LibreOffice tdf128596); pictures keep stretching. Slide backgrounds, group fills and table cells tile at the picture's own size too (LibreOffice tdf152070: a 300 dpi tile at 60 x 70 %).

<a id="line.basic"></a>

### Outline width and color

`line.basic` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** SVG stroke; widths are in EMU (12700 per point); inset alignment clips a doubled stroke.

**Office behaviour:** PowerPoint draws an outline in whole pixels, at least one (round(W px); measured with local/lw-probe and local/cap-probe, outlines of 0.25 to 6 pt at quarter- and eighth-pixel offsets). On a shape that is not turned, an axis-aligned outline's every edge is rounded to the nearest pixel boundary on its own (half up), the stroke covering floor(n / 2) pixels before it and the rest after; any other path is stretched with its box, whose edges are rounded so, and only moved along a side that would round to nothing (LibreOffice's fdo47434: connectors sloping by 0.02 px keep their slope). A curved path's outline of two pixels or more misses one of PowerPoint's antialiasing samples on its left and top sides: the first pixel there is 7/8 covered along a row and 3/4 down a column, also on round-capped lines (sd-data/theme 88.9 → 98.6 %). Nearly level or upright straight lines are antialiased where PowerPoint's samples see them, as table borders are (table.cells). Word's drawings keep their exact widths and places. Verified with generated/lines and outlines (99.0 %).

**Elements (1):** [`a:ln`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aln)

**Specification:** §20.1.2.2.24 Outline (p.2755)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1212

<a id="line.dash"></a>

### Dash styles

`line.dash` · P1 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** stroke-dasharray in multiples of the line width.

**Office behaviour:** Preset dashes and custom patterns (a:custDash, a:ds: dash and space in thousandths of a percent of the line width) repeat in units of the line's width in whole pixels (measured: a 4 pt line, 5.33 px, repeats a 3:1 pattern every 20 px; a 1.5 pt line every 8 px). On a closed figure the pattern starts where the closing segment starts, the figure's last point, and runs through the closing segment first: a rectangle starts at its bottom left corner and runs up, a triangle at its bottom right corner, an ellipse at its left (measured with probe decks of one long dash on 24 presets and on freeforms); open figures start at their first point. Round caps lie within the dashes, and a round dash is at least one width long (a 0.5:2 pattern repeats every 3 widths); on closed figures the dashes cover [0, d) of the pattern, on open lines they lie half a width earlier, where the line's cap begins. Square caps do not cap the dashes, which end flat at their full length: only an open line's start gets the cap, and its end when it lies within a dash. Measured with generated/lines-dash (five custom patterns at 1.5, 4 and 8 pt on lines, rectangles and ellipses with flat, round and square caps): 89.7 %, the rest a pixel at the ends of the dashes and the drift of PowerPoint's curves along ellipses. Word starts the pattern elsewhere in its documents' drawings: on a rectangle that is not turned, which it writes as a PDF rectangle, at the bottom left corner along the bottom, on every other figure at its first point (measured by agent BK on Word's PDFs; LibreOffice's extras-ooxmlexport/fdo73247, a rectangle turned 33 degrees, from its top left corner to the right, 89.2 → 97.1 %; textbox_picturefill 91.4 → 96.5 %, LineStyle_DashType 98.0 → 99.5 %, shape-theme-preservation 96.7 → 100 %).

**Elements (3):** [`a:custDash`](../format/namespaces/a.md#acustdash), [`a:ds`](../format/namespaces/a.md#ads), [`a:prstDash`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aprstdash)

**Specification:** §20.1.8.21 Custom Dash (p.2870); §20.1.8.22 Dash Stop (p.2871); §20.1.8.48 Preset Dash (p.2886)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1292

<a id="line.joinCap"></a>

### Joins and caps

`line.joinCap` · P1 · difficulty 1 · done

**Rendering:** stroke-linejoin, stroke-miterlimit and stroke-linecap.

**Office behaviour:** Verified with generated/lines and outlines.

<a id="line.ends"></a>

### Arrowheads

`line.ends` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Triangle, stealth, diamond, oval and open arrow heads as paths sized relative to the line width, with the line shortened under the head.

**Office behaviour:** Heads are multiples of the line width but never smaller than a 2 pt line's (measured: medium heads 6 pt wide on lines of 0.25 to 2 pt, three widths beyond; LibreOffice's fdo47434). A head points along the nearest leg that has a length; on an arc along its tangent, the arc pulled back along itself (LibreOffice's tdf113187_arcTo_withoutReferences 57.7 → 90.1 %). Verified with generated/lines (arrowhead types, widths and lengths).

**Elements (2):** [`a:headEnd`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aheadend), [`a:tailEnd`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atailend)

**Specification:** §20.1.8.38 Line Head/End Style (p.2880); §20.1.8.57 Tail line end style (p.2892)

<a id="line.compound"></a>

### Compound lines

`line.compound` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

a:ln/@cmpd (double, thick-thin, thin-thick, triple).

**Rendering:** Several parallel strokes or a stroke with a mask.

<a id="line.fill"></a>

### Gradient and pattern outlines

`line.fill` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

gradFill and pattFill inside a:ln.

**Rendering:** SVG gradient or pattern as stroke paint.

**Office behaviour:** Gradient outlines take the gradient over the shape's box like fills (arrowheads too); pattern outlines draw the pattern itself, its 8 px tiles aligned to the slide like pattern fills (generated/outlines).

### Effect lists

<a id="effect.outerShadow"></a>

### Outer shadow

`effect.outerShadow` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** SVG filter with blur (blurRad), offset (dist, dir), scaling, skew and alignment; CSS drop-shadow where it suffices.

**Office behaviour:** A shadow is the shape scaled and skewed about its alignment point (algn, bottom center by default) with [sx, tan kx; tan ky, sy], the skew not scaled, then moved by dist along dir and blurred (σ = 0.35 blurRad). With rotWithShape 0, as PowerPoint writes its shadows, the offset and the transform stay on the slide, about the box around the turned shape (measured with generated/effects). Verified with generated/effects and probe/shadow, probe/rotshadow. The outline's outer half casts its shadow too (docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/n705956-2: a 3 pt outline's shadow was cut at the box), and so does geometry beyond the shape's box (docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/ tdf41466_testVmlShapeWithTextbox: a callout's tail 40 pt beside its box, 85.4 → 94.2 %).

**Elements (1):** [`a:outerShdw`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aoutershdw)

**Specification:** §20.1.8.45 Outer Shadow Effect (p.2884)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1303

<a id="effect.innerShadow"></a>

### Inner shadow

`effect.innerShadow` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** SVG filter inside the shape's alpha.

**Office behaviour:** The shape's outside, blurred (σ = 0.35 blurRad) and moved against dir, colored and kept inside the shape; the filter region reaches beyond the shape by the distance and the blur so that the outside exists to cast it. Verified with generated/effects and local/innershadow (offsets 3 to 12 pt, blur 0 to 8 pt: 100 %).

**Elements (1):** [`a:innerShdw`](../format/namespaces/a.md#ainnershdw)

**Specification:** §20.1.8.40 Inner Shadow Effect (p.2882)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1300

`effect.presetShadow` · P3 · difficulty 3 · done

**Office behaviour:** Verified with generated/effects.

<a id="effect.glow"></a>

### Glow

`effect.glow` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Dilate and blur the shape's alpha, colored.

**Office behaviour:** Verified with generated/effects. The glow grows from the shape's whole silhouette, also where its fill is mostly transparent (Word's PDF of LibreOffice's extras-ooxmlexport/fdo66688: a 5 pt glow around a white fill of 20 % alpha as strong as around an opaque one, 86.7 → 99.9 %). It grows round, as a disc dilates: corners and slanted edges no further out than straight ones (measured in Word's PDFs, docx effect.glow; local/textfx5 99.3 → 99.9 %, generated/objects 98.8 → 99.3 %, generated/effects 99.2 → 99.5 %, generated/text-effects 96.1 → 96.4 %); SVG dilates by rectangles only, so the union of four whose corners lie on the circle.

**Elements (1):** [`a:glow`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aglow)

**Specification:** §20.1.8.32 Glow Effect (p.2878)

`effect.softEdge` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

**Office behaviour:** Verified with generated/effects.

**Specification:** §20.1.8.53 Soft Edge Effect (p.2890)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1306

<a id="effect.reflection"></a>

### Reflection

`effect.reflection` · P2 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Mirrored copy with distance, blur and a gradient alpha mask.

**Office behaviour:** The copy is scaled and skewed like a shadow about algn, moved by dist along dir, faded along fadeDir (90° by default) across the copy from stA at stPos to endA at endPos and blurred; with rotWithShape 0 the turned shape is mirrored on the slide (measured with generated/effects). Verified with generated/effects and probe/refl.

**Elements (1):** [`a:reflection`](../format/namespaces/a.md#areflection)

<a id="effect.blur"></a>

### Blur and fill overlay

`effect.blur` · P3 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** SVG Gaussian blur; fill overlay blends a fill over the shape.

**Office behaviour:** Blur: a Gaussian blur with sigma = rad / 3 (measured); without grow the result is cut at the outline's outer edge, with grow it spreads beyond. Fill overlay: the overlay fill is blended over the shape's fill only, not its outline (over, mult, screen, darken, lighten as CSS blend modes in an isolated group).

**Elements (2):** [`a:blur`](../format/namespaces/a.md#ablur), [`a:fillOverlay`](../format/namespaces/a.md#afilloverlay)

**Specification:** §20.1.8.15 Blur Effect (p.2868); §20.1.8.29 Fill Overlay Effect (p.2875)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1289

<a id="effect.dag"></a>

### Effect containers (effect DAG)

`effect.dag` · P3 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Effect graphs with containers and blends; rare outside themes from Office 2007.

**Office behaviour:** PowerPoint draws an effect graph instead of the shape (measured with generated/effects-dag, 97.2 %): its input is the whole shape with its text; a container of type sib draws its children's outputs over each other, type tree feeds each child the previous output; an empty container draws nothing, while a graph without any effect leaves the shape as it is. a:effect ref brings in the fill, the line, fill and line with the text (fillLine), or a named container's output. Every effect outputs only its own result: an outer shadow or glow without the shape, a reflection without the original, alphaOutset grows the alpha with black, fill paints the input's alpha (gradients through a mask), relOff and xfrm move it, the color effects work as on pictures. blend draws its container's output over the input in a blend mode, over being overlay (measured to two levels), mult multiply and so on; fillOverlay likewise with a fill. Drawn as nested SVG groups, filters, masks and mix-blend-mode.

**Elements (7):** [`a:alphaOutset`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aalphaoutset), [`a:blend`](../format/namespaces/a.md#ablend), [`a:cont`](../format/namespaces/a.md#acont), [`a:effect`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aeffect), [`a:effectDag`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aeffectdag), [`a:fill`](../format/namespaces/a.md#afill), [`a:relOff`](../format/namespaces/a.md#areloff)

**Specification:** §20.1.4.2.7 Effect (p.2816); §20.1.4.2.9 Fill (p.2817); §20.1.8.7 Alpha Inset/Outset Effect (p.2863); §20.1.8.12 Blend Effect (p.2865); §20.1.8.20 Effect Container (p.2870); §20.1.8.24 Effect (p.2871) and 3 more

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1263, 2.1.1291, 2.1.1294, 2.1.1295

<a id="effect.scene3d"></a>

### 3D scene (camera and light)

`effect.scene3d` · P2 · difficulty 5 · done

**Rendering:** The camera turns and projects the shape: a CSS matrix3d for the front face with its text, SVG polygons for the body. The light rig lights every face with Lambert diffuse and Phong highlights, fitted per rig.

**Office behaviour:** Measured on generated/three-d: the presets' angles and fields of view (30 cameras, corners within 0.4 pt; the eye stands 907.5 pt / 2 / tan(fov / 2) in front of the shape); oblique presets shear by 0.3 of the depth (legacy ones 0.5), legacy perspective presets by 0.133 per axis. Legacy oblique cameras with a rotation of their own turn the scene before they shear it, their light rig staying with the viewer under the turns about the horizontal and vertical axes and turning along in the picture plane (LibreOffice's Scene3d_legacyObliqueBottomRight 51.5 to 97.3 %, Scene3d_lightRig_legacyCamera 41.2 to 98.4 %); how modern oblique cameras turn is not measured yet (Scene3d_obliqueTopRight). All 27 light rigs were fitted on extruded cubes, cylinders and tilted bevels rendered in two base colors (rms below 0.03, brightRoom 0.05); dir turns a rig in 45° steps clockwise, a:rot turns it further. sp3d z moves the shape along its own axis (perspective enlarges it, turned cameras shift it). Shadows and glow of 3D shapes come from the body's back as the camera sees it (the glow half as wide), soft edges do not apply. A backdrop (a:backdrop) takes the shadow and glow instead: the flat shape's picture lies in that plane with its x along up × normal and its y along the up vector, its corner at the anchor less half the shape's size (scene coordinates as DrawingML's: y down, z towards the viewer), the shadow moved by its distance along the scene's x and y, behind the body when the plane lies behind it, else over its sides (fitted to glow and shadows on walls and floors of both orientations with perspective and isometric cameras within 3 px; local/backdrop, backdrop2, backdrop3: 98.3 to 98.6 %). The shape's text casts its shadow there too, over the shape's own and darker where they overlap, as its plain glyphs without effects of their own (local/bdtext: a floor plane shows the text in the shadow). Oblique cameras turn the scene by their rotation (a:rot) before they shift it; the shape's own rotation turns the projected picture, its depths' shift along, the faces lit as unturned, where the other cameras turn the shape in its plane (local/oblique-rot: an extruded box under obliqueTopRight turned by lat, lon and rev alone and together, every variant 99.7 to 100 %; LibreOffice's oox/Scene3d_obliqueTopRight 36.0 → 99.9 %).

**Elements (8):** [`a:anchor`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aanchor), [`a:backdrop`](../format/namespaces/a.md#abackdrop), [`a:camera`](../format/namespaces/a.md#acamera), [`a:lightRig`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alightrig), [`a:norm`](../format/namespaces/a.md#anorm), [`a:rot`](../format/namespaces/a.md#arot), [`a:scene3d`](../format/namespaces/a.md#ascene3d), [`a:up`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aup)

**Specification:** §20.1.4.1.26 3D Scene Properties (p.2809); §20.1.5.1 Anchor Point (p.2833); §20.1.5.2 Backdrop Plane (p.2834); §20.1.5.5 Camera (p.2837); §20.1.5.9 Light Rig (p.2841); §20.1.5.10 Normal (p.2842) and 2 more

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1266, 2.1.1267, 2.1.1270, 2.1.1274, 2.1.1275, 2.1.1277

<a id="effect.shape3d"></a>

### 3D shape (bevel, extrusion, material)

`effect.shape3d` · P2 · difficulty 5 · done

**Rendering:** Bevels (top and bottom, measured profiles), extrusion, back face and contour are built as SVG polygons, sorted back to front and lit with the rig and the material; the front face is drawn by the camera.

**Office behaviour:** Measured on generated/three-d: bevel profiles from edge-on silhouettes (they scale with width and height independently; a bevel is at most half the smaller side wide), relaxedInset with the groove its shading shows behind its crest, 0.4 of the height lower from 0.8 of the width on (found by an agent in Word's pictures of 3D text; generated/three-d, tables-more and the backdrop decks a little closer); the front face lies in the shape's plane, the top bevel's outline its height behind it, then the extrusion and the bottom bevel. The shape's line is part of the body: the body reaches to the line's outer edge, the sides take the line's color unless extrusionClr is set, and so do the outer band of the bevels and of the back. The contour is an unlit tube of contourW (black by default) around the rims, creases and silhouettes; a face coming towards the eye covers all but r·cos α of its inner half, and the body moves back so that the tube does not reach in front of the shape's plane. Materials were fitted against threePt, the latest on cubes from 25 sides on black and gray slides (local/cubes-*, within 1 to 3.5 levels rms): matte without highlights, plastic and metal with stronger and tighter ones, metals partly tinted, the legacy materials' and the powders' from the half vector; metals, dkEdge, softEdge and the powders fade towards the silhouette, metals, softEdge and the powders whiten there ((1 − n·v)^3.8 for metal and softEdge), powder mixes half white into the surface color; flat ignores the light's direction; legacyWireframe draws every mesh edge, hidden ones included. clear and translucentPowder are translucent, the same in every channel: clear most transparent facing the eye (opacity 1 − 0.9 n·v), translucentPowder towards the silhouette (0.7 n·v); only faces towards the eye are drawn, the highlights add in full (the color brightens by highlight / opacity and saturates at white) and text on the front face stays opaque (local/clear, clearA-C). Without a body (no extrusion, bevel or contour) the powders show warmMatte's light with their broad highlights only, no pastel look and nothing towards the silhouette (local/powder, matflat1-2). Pictures and gradients keep their texture on the bevel. An extruded frame (a shape with a hole) shows the walls of its hole (LibreOffice's Scene3d_lightRig_*), and PowerPoint draws a wireframe in the line's color and width (Scene3d_material_wireframe). A body without fill has no faces of its own: no bevels and no back, its sides only in the extrusion's or the line's color (seen from within too where the front is open), and its line and text cast its shadow and glow as on a flat shape; without either it shows nothing (local/nofill3d; LibreOffice's sd-data/n819614, a SmartArt shape with noFill, no line, a bevel and a shadow, 89.9 → 93.5 %). The back of a body seen from behind shows its fill, the line's color only on its outer band (the band and the fill sorted together); under twoPt it takes the ambient light alone, 0.25 of its fill, under threePt and balanced the rigs' lights (local/backlight: donuts seen from behind under six rigs and turns; LibreOffice's oox/Scene3d_material_highlight 64.3 → 95.8 %).

**Elements (6):** [`a:bevel`](../format/namespaces/a.md#abevel), [`a:bevelB`](../format/namespaces/a.md#abevelb), [`a:bevelT`](../format/namespaces/a.md#abevelt), [`a:contourClr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#acontourclr), [`a:extrusionClr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aextrusionclr), [`a:sp3d`](../format/namespaces/a.md#asp3d)

`picture.basic` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

A picture without its picture (an a:blip naming none, or a part the package lacks) shows PowerPoint's sign: a 1-pixel #6D6D6D frame on its edge, the broken-picture icon 6 pixels in and "The picture can't be displayed." in Office's language ("Das Bild kann nicht angezeigt werden."; Apache POI's missing-blip-fill, LibreOffice's tdf147586); the player words it in the viewer's language.

<a id="picture.formats"></a>

### Raster image formats

`picture.formats` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

The content type behind a:blip/@r:embed.

**Rendering:** PNG, JPEG, GIF, BMP, SVG and WebP natively (also when the package names another content type); TIFF decoded (strips or tiles; uncompressed, PackBits, LZW, Deflate with prediction, CCITT group 3/4, JPEG; bilevel, gray, palette, RGB(A), CMYK; 1-16 bit) and converted to PNG, JPEG-compressed TIFF merged into one JPEG (Huffman re-coding where strips use different tables); color profiles carried along; JPEG XR decoded by our own decoder after ITU-T T.832 (spatial and frequency order, tiles, all overlap modes, 4:2:0, 4:2:2 and 4:4:4, gray, CMYK, n-channel, 1 to 32-bit integer, fixed and floating point, RGBE, interleaved and separate alpha, orientation) and converted to PNG.

**Office behaviour:** PowerPoint keeps TIFF pictures as inserted (also CCITT and JPEG compressed ones) and honors embedded ICC profiles (PNG, JPEG, TIFF; verified with a swapped-primaries profile); profile-less CMYK shows through a 9^4 table measured from PowerPoint's rendering (harvest-cmyk). Pattern tiles of stretched pictures reach a margin with the edge pixels repeated, so edges no longer blend with the opposite edge. Inserted .jxr/.wdp pictures stay JPEG XR, but PowerPoint names .jxr parts image/png (the extension's default), so pictures are told apart by their bytes. The JPEG XR decoder matches Windows' own (WIC) sample for sample on 44 files of its encoder in all its options (frequency and spatial order, tiles, overlap 0-2, subsampling, quality, without flexbits, highpass or lowpass, separate and interleaved alpha, rotations; gray, bilevel, 565, 555, 101010, 16-bit, float, CMYK): the highpass prediction direction comes from the lowpass coefficients before dequantization (the order of steps in 9.4 would take them after), centred chroma is upsampled with the taps (-2, 9, 28, -3) / 32 (4:2:0 in both directions before one rounding), packed formats swap red and blue unless RED_BLUE_NOT_SWAPPED. Only WIC's own 8-bit conversion of 10-bit, 16-bit and floating point samples differs by 1 at most. hdphoto parts of artistic effects (a14:imgLayer) are originals; the blip holds the rendered result. Deck: picture-formats 99.9 % (slide 2: ten JPEG XR pictures). The bytes count, not the name: a PNG stored as .emf (image/x-emf) shows as the PNG (LibreOffice's graphic-stroke).

<a id="picture.metafiles"></a>

### EMF and WMF pictures

`picture.metafiles` · P1 · difficulty 5 · done

**Rendering:** Our own EMF/EMF+/WMF interpreter that emits SVG ([MS-EMF], [MS-EMFPLUS], [MS-WMF]).

**Office behaviour:** EMF and WMF are frequent in real decks (icons, content pasted from Excel or Visio, older charts). The interpreter draws the GDI records (lines, curves, PolyDraw, shapes, paths, regions, clipping with OffsetClipRgn and meta regions, world and page transforms, text, BitBlt, StretchBlt, MaskBlt, PlgBlt, DIB transfers, AlphaBlend, TransparentBlt, gradient fills, hatch and pattern brushes in output pixels) and the EMF+ records of dual and EMF+ only files (fills and outlines with solid, hatch, texture, linear and path gradient brushes, paths, regions, images, strings, driver strings, containers, transforms, clips). Logical palettes are played: CreatePalette, SelectPalette, SetPaletteEntries, ResizePalette and AnimatePalette, PALETTEINDEX colors and DIB_PAL_COLORS bitmaps against the selected palette (the default one has the 20 static colors), PALETTERGB as its own color (local/palette, EMF and WMF). Monochrome pattern brushes (EMF CreateMonoBrush, WMF DIBCREATEPATTERNBRUSH with BS_PATTERN) paint in the text and background colors as GDI does, not in their palette's black and white (measured with the previews of ActiveX toggle buttons and scroll bars, whose dithers PowerPoint draws in the control's colors). A bitmap's negative width or height reaches from its origin the other way, its first column or row at the origin, as in GDI's StretchBlt (LibreOffice's tdf149206: an EMF named .wmf whose screenshot sits 750 x -600 under a window turned upside down, 29.2 to 83.5 %). EMF text aligned by its top or bottom (TA_TOP, TA_BOTTOM) stands by GDI's ascent and descent of the font realized in whole pixels of the metafile's device (lfHeight -ppem): the VDMX record of the size for the Windows ANSI character set, else usWinAscent and usWinDescent scaled and rounded (measured with GDI's GetTextMetrics: Arial, Times New Roman, Courier New, Segoe UI and Verdana by their VDMX, Calibri and Cambria rounded, Tahoma's VDMX for all glyphs not used; Word's PDFs: Arial at 13 px 13 px below the reference point, Calibri at 15 px 14 px, LibreOffice's tdf99631 in Word 89.4 → 98.8 %; PowerPoint: poi/at.ecodesign.www_downloads_Vertiefungsvortrag_elektronik slides 21 and 25 0.4 and 1.6 points better). WMF text keeps the unrounded ascent (not measured). 8-bit text reads in its font's character set: Cyrillic (204) as Windows-1251, Central European (238) as 1250 and so on, symbol fonts byte by byte, the rest as Windows-1252 (Word's PDF of LibreOffice's fdo58949: Equation.3 previews with lfCharSet 204 print "НВВ", 83.9 → 95.8 %). Flood fills are not drawn, as PowerPoint draws none (measured); escapes draw nothing, color management records leave the colors as they are, and OpenGL records are not drawn. Verified with generated/metafiles (PowerPoint's EMF and WMF exports, GDI+ dual and GDI-only recordings, GDI records: 96.2 %; hatches come out smoothed in Chromium where GDI sets whole pixels). In WMF too an icon's monochrome mask ANDed (SRCAND) and its image XORed at the same place (SRCINVERT) make one image, opaque where the mask is black, as in EMF (tika's testWPSAttachment: nine OLE icons were black boxes, 72.9 → 90.6 %; poi/bug64693 92.4 → 94.5 %).

<a id="picture.media"></a>

### Audio and video

`picture.media` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** HTML5 video/audio with the picture's blip as poster image; trimming and fades in the player; caption tracks as WebVTT.

**Office behaviour:** Bookmarks (p14:bmkLst) travel with the media element and start the trigger sequences that wait for them (p14:bmkTgt, evt onMediaBookmark); caption and subtitle tracks (p173:tracksInfo) become WebVTT text tracks of the video; a file's declared media type (contentType) is kept. CD audio (a:audioCd with its a:st and a:end track times) cannot play from a document. Online videos (a:videoFile linked to a web player such as YouTube's embed page) play in a frame that loads the player only when it plays; YouTube refuses to play in a page opened from a file (error 153), so the presenter app serves the document over localhost. Verified with generated/media (99.4 %).

<a id="picture.ink"></a>

### Ink

`picture.ink` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Use the mc:Fallback picture first; later draw the InkML traces as SVG paths with pressure-dependent width.

**Office behaviour:** Ink (p:contentPart, Office 2010 markup chosen over the fallback picture) is drawn from its InkML or InkActionML part (strokes an action removed left out): the smallest trace coordinates go to the part's top left corner; a part as large as its points plus the last brush's width (its natural size, as PowerPoint writes it) keeps the ink's scale, any other size stretches the points to fill it; stroke widths never scale; a part with a highlighter (rasterOp maskPen) is half transparent as a whole. With a pressure channel (F) the width follows the pen: (0.25 + 1.5 F/max) times the brush width, tapering smoothly between points, ignorePressure keeps the brush width (measured with generated/ink). fitToCurve: PowerPoint draws such a stroke as cubic Beziers fitted to its points, one piece for up to five points, with corners where the fit breaks (a stroke following the pen's pressure as a polyline through the points moved onto that curve). It is drawn here as the uniform cubic B-spline of the points with its ends held: approximating like PowerPoint's fit, corners cut inwards, no loops or overshoots; reversals stay corners. Along dense ink both stay within half a pixel of the polyline; on sparse points PowerPoint's fit reaches further (a three-point V keeps its depth, a zigzag of five becomes one U; local/inkfit, inkfit2-4). Verified with generated/ink (98.8 %) and probe/ink.

<a id="picture.model3d"></a>

### 3D models

`picture.model3d` · P2 · difficulty 1 · done

**Rendering:** Show PowerPoint's picture of the model (mc:Fallback); while the slide show animates the model, our own glTF 2.0 reader and WebGL2 renderer (model3d.js, only in documents with models) draws it on a canvas over the picture, lit and tone mapped like PowerPoint.

**Office behaviour:** PowerPoint stores the model as GLB (am3d:model3d r:embed) with a rendered picture of it (am3d:raster, Office3DRenderer, 2 px per point, also the mc:Fallback picture). The camera (pos, up, lookAt in EMU, 36,000,000 EMU to the meter) looks at the model; a perspective's fov spans the square objViewport (viewportSz) about the frame's center, of which the frame shows its middle, and nothing is drawn outside that square. The model's transform: model units to meters (meterPerModelUnit), preTrans, scale, rotation about x, then y, then z (60000ths of a degree), postTrans. Lighting, measured with generated spheres and mirror balls: a studio environment (a room with windows, reconstructed from the mirror balls) for diffuse light and reflections (split sum, GGX), the slide's point lights (candela, inverse square, linear scRGB colors) and its ambient light; emissive counts half; base color factors are kept in 8 bits; the result goes through PowerPoint's tone curve (s = 1 - exp(-(2.358 u^0.666 + 1.886 u)) per channel, u in the units in which an unlit material shows its base color). Our renders of calibration spheres and boxes are within 1 to 5 of 255 on average; contact shadows (ambient occlusion) are not drawn.

<a id="text"></a>

## Text

Text decides the fidelity of most slides. The engine measures glyphs with the real fonts and breaks lines itself; the browser only draws positioned runs.

<a id="text.body"></a>

### Text body and text box

`text.body` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** The text box is the geometry's text rectangle minus the insets (default 91440 EMU left/right, 45720 top/bottom); anchoring top, middle or bottom, anchorCtr, wrap or no wrap, rotation and overflow.

**Office behaviour:** anchorCtr centers the smallest box around the text, the paragraphs keep their alignment within it; vertOverflow ellipsis and clip drop the lines that do not fit whole (ellipsis ends the last line kept with …, whole words removed) and the rest is anchored as usual; horzOverflow clip cuts at the text rectangle, not at the insets; spcFirstLastPara keeps the space before the first and after the last paragraph; compatLnSpc shows no difference (measured with generated/text-body). Compatible line spacing (compatLnSpc) leaves PowerPoint's lines where they are (measured: 150 % spacing unchanged); forceAA only asks for anti-aliasing. A text area narrower or lower than its insets collapses to its middle, x + (w + lIns - rIns) / 2 (measured with the object model: a box 5 pt wide with insets of 10 and 2 pt sets every line at 6.5 pt whatever its alignment; a box 1 pt high 0.5 pt below its top). A placeholder 0 wide does not wrap (one line in footer, body and title placeholders), while text boxes, shapes and placeholders 1 pt wide wrap every character (LibreOffice's 0-width-text-wrap). Verified with generated/text-body (97.7 %), text-more and objects-more. With anchorCtr a text block wider than its area (no wrap, long words) is centered too, reaching out on both sides.

**Elements (6):** [`a:bodyPr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#abodypr), [`a:txBody`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atxbody), [`a:txSp`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atxsp), [`a:useSpRect`](../format/namespaces/a.md#ausesprect), [`dsp:txXfrm`](../format/namespaces/dsp.md#dsptxxfrm), [`p:txBody`](../format/namespaces/p.md#ptxbody)

**Specification:** §19.3.1.51 Shape Text Body (p.2603); §20.1.2.2.40 Shape Text Body (p.2764); §20.1.2.2.41 Text Shape (p.2764); §20.1.2.2.42 Use Shape Text Rectangle (p.2765); §21.1.2.1.1 Body Properties (p.3197)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1379

<a id="text.autofit"></a>

### Autofit

`text.autofit` · P0 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Apply the stored fontScale and lnSpcReduction; with spAutoFit the stored shape size already fits the text.

**Office behaviour:** PowerPoint saves the shrink factors it computed when the slide was last edited; recomputing them yields different line breaks than PowerPoint shows. Without stored factors it does not shrink at all, even when normAutofit is inherited and the text overflows (verified with generated/text-basic, slide 5). The scaled sizes are whole points, halves rounded up: 26 pt at 90 % is laid out at 23 pt and 25 pt at 23 pt, 18 pt at 62.5 % at 11 pt (the object model reports these sizes; generated/text-more, slide 5). Verified with generated/text-basic, text-body and text-more.

**Elements (3):** [`a:noAutofit`](../format/namespaces/a.md#anoautofit), [`a:normAutofit`](../format/namespaces/a.md#anormautofit), [`a:spAutoFit`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aspautofit)

**Specification:** §21.1.2.1.2 No AutoFit (p.3205); §21.1.2.1.3 Normal AutoFit (p.3206); §21.1.2.1.4 Shape AutoFit (p.3207)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1380, 2.1.1381, 2.1.1382

### Paragraphs

`text.paragraph` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Alignment (left, center, right, justified, distributed), margins, first-line or hanging indent, levels 0–8.

**Office behaviour:** fontAlgn t, ctr and b line up the tops, middles or bottoms of runs of different sizes (base and auto: the baselines; measured with generated/text-body). A word longer than the line breaks between characters, and its trailing spaces hang at the end of its last piece as after any word (measured: "縦書き 2024年 AB" in mongolianVert columns one character long takes no column for its spaces, generated/text-more slide 3). <a id="text.spacing"></a>

### Line and paragraph spacing

`text.spacing` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Line spacing as a percentage of the line height or in points; space before and after.

**Office behaviour:** Measured with the text-metrics calibration deck (9 fonts, multiples, exact spacing, sizes): single line height is 1.2 × the font size for every font, multiples scale it, exact spacing is rounded to whole points. At single spacing the baseline sits at 1.2 × size × ascent/(ascent+descent) (typo metrics if USE_TYPO_METRICS, else Windows metrics); above single spacing at H × min(0.75, ratio); below at max(S × ratio − (S − H), H × min(0.75, ratio)). Lines in fonts of one ratio take the largest ascent and descent of their runs. Where the ratios differ, a line is as high as the line of its largest font, and its baseline divides that height as the largest ascent and descent of its fonts at single spacing divide theirs, among them the line break ending it, or on the paragraph's last line its mark (a:endParaRPr, the theme's font where it names none; without a:endParaRPr the last run's Latin font), each in its own font at the size of the run before it, its own size counting for nothing (measured in PowerPoint's PDF of local/mixfonts-a to -d: two and three fonts at one size and at two, at 80, 100 and 150 % and exactly 36 pt, breaks and marks of fonts and sizes of their own, Chinese and Latin text, all within 0.1 pt; on screen 307 of 322 lines on PowerPoint's pixel rows, the rest one or two rows off; the 37 decks it changes 95.86 -> 95.99 %, LibreOffice's tdf129686 79.0 -> 97.4 % and tdf98603 89.1 -> 96.4 %). Bullets do not count. Space before is ignored for the first paragraph; percentages of space before/after refer to 1.2 × font size. Glyph origins are snapped to device pixels. Verified with generated/text-metrics (98.9 %).

**Elements (5):** [`a:lnSpc`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alnspc), [`a:spcAft`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aspcaft), [`a:spcBef`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aspcbef), [`a:spcPct`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aspcpct), [`a:spcPts`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aspcpts)

**Specification:** §21.1.2.2.5 Line Spacing (p.3224); §21.1.2.2.9 Space After (p.3236); §21.1.2.2.10 Space Before (p.3236); §21.1.2.2.11 Spacing Percent (p.3237); §21.1.2.2.12 Spacing Points (p.3238)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1386, 2.1.1389, 2.1.1390, 2.1.1391

<a id="text.tabs"></a>

### Tab stops

`text.tabs` · P1 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Left, center, right and decimal tabs, default tab size from defTabSz.

**Office behaviour:** Tab stops (a:tabLst, inherited through the list styles like the other paragraph properties) are measured from the text area's left edge, the origin of marL: left stops put the text there, center stops center it, right stops end it there and decimal stops put its decimal point there (text without one ends at the stop); text never moves left of where it already is; past the last custom stop the default stops continue at multiples of defTabSz; a hanging first line (negative indent) stops at the margin first. Measured with generated/text-tabs (default spacings, every stop kind, margins and indents, bullets, centered and right-aligned paragraphs). A tab binds to the word after it: a word that does not fit after the tab's stop takes the tab to the next line, and a tab that starts a line keeps what fits of the word after its stop, possibly nothing (measured with the object model's lines in a 385 pt line of 20 pt Arial: "Text⇥after" with a stop at 380 pt gives "Text", a line with only the tab and "after …"; with a stop at 350 pt "Text", "⇥afte", "r …"; a stop past the line's end leaves the tab alone on its line; a second tab moves to the next line with its word and stops at the first stop there).

**Elements (2):** [`a:tab`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atab), [`a:tabLst`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atablst)

**Specification:** §21.1.2.2.13 Tab Stop (p.3239); §21.1.2.2.14 Tab List (p.3240)

<a id="text.bullets"></a>

### Bullets and numbering

`text.bullets` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Bullet glyph in its own font (Wingdings and Symbol mapped to Unicode or embedded), color and size; automatic numbering in 40 schemes (arabic, roman, alphabetic, circled, East Asian, Hebrew, Arabic, Thai …) with startAt; picture bullets.

**Office behaviour:** A hanging indent reaching left of the text area moves the paragraph right (marL 0, indent -27 pt: bullet at 0, text at 27 pt). With a first line indented further than the margin the bullet stays at the margin and the text starts at the indent (object model: marL 0 and indent 27 pt start the text at 27 pt; marL 36 and indent 0 right after the bullet; LibreOffice's bullet-indent). Numbering continues while the paragraphs before at the level count in the same scheme from the same start, and begins anew with another scheme or start (LibreOffice's n90255: 3 4, II III, C D, iv v, f) g) for pairs of paragraphs with five schemes). Latin letters repeat after z (aa, bb); Arabic schemes count in hijā'ī (arabic1) and abjad (arabic2) order followed by a hyphen-minus; Thai letters skip ฃ ฅ ฆ. Picture bullets are 0.7 of the text's size high in any font, as wide as their proportions make them, and sit on the baseline; text after one wider than the indent starts right after it (PowerPoint's PDF of LibreOffice's tdf114913 in Calibri, Arial and Times New Roman at 14 to 44 pt). A bullet character is the first character of buChar/@char (LibreOffice's bnc862510_5 and tdf93830 write two). Verified with generated/lists (all 41 numbering schemes, picture and character bullets).

**Elements (11):** [`a:buAutoNum`](../format/namespaces/a.md#abuautonum), [`a:buBlip`](../format/namespaces/a.md#abublip), [`a:buChar`](../format/namespaces/a.md#abuchar), [`a:buClr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#abuclr), [`a:buClrTx`](../format/namespaces/a.md#abuclrtx), [`a:buFont`](../format/namespaces/a.md#abufont), [`a:buFontTx`](../format/namespaces/a.md#abufonttx), [`a:buNone`](../format/namespaces/a.md#abunone), [`a:buSzPct`](../format/namespaces/a.md#abuszpct), [`a:buSzPts`](../format/namespaces/a.md#abuszpts), [`a:buSzTx`](../format/namespaces/a.md#abusztx)

**Specification:** §21.1.2.4.1 Auto-Numbered Bullet (p.3268); §21.1.2.4.2 Picture Bullet (p.3269); §21.1.2.4.3 Character Bullet (p.3271); §21.1.2.4.4 Color Specified (p.3272); §21.1.2.4.5 Follow Text (p.3273); §21.1.2.4.6 Specified (p.3274) and 5 more

<a id="text.styles"></a>

### List styles and the text style cascade

`text.styles` · P0 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Run → paragraph → the shape's lstStyle → layout placeholder → master placeholder → the master's title or body style for title and body placeholders, the presentation's defaultTextStyle for all other text; the shape's fontRef adds color and font. Text outside title and body placeholders skips the master's other style except for its color.

**Office behaviour:** Measured with the object model, otherStyle level 1 at 10 pt bold red Courier New right-aligned with 150 % spacing and defaultTextStyle at 30 pt italic blue Georgia centered with 80 %: text boxes, shapes and dt, ftr and sldNum placeholders without a layout placeholder take size, italics, font, spacing, alignment, indent and line spacing from defaultTextStyle and only the color from otherStyle (not its bold); table cells take otherStyle. LibreOffice's TextOnCircle (otherStyle 15.12 pt, defaultTextStyle 18 pt) rises from 43 to 93 %. Title and body placeholders take nothing from defaultTextStyle (POI's sample and alterman_security: titles whose style has no kern are not kerned, which the default style's kern="1200" would do; 30 such decks agree). A master without p:txStyles, or without its title or body style, has PowerPoint's own, Office's default ones: titles at 44 pt in the theme's heading font, the levels below the first unset; body text at 28, 24, 20 and 18 pt with bullets (• in Arial), indents of 18 pt plus 36 pt a level, 10 and 5 pt before; all kerned from 12 pt at 90 % line spacing (measured with the object model and PowerPoint's PDF on variants of LibreOffice's tdf160487); an empty style the master has is used as it is (tdf165341: 18 pt). Verified with generated/text-basic, text-body and presentation.

**Elements (17):** [`a:defPPr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#adefppr), [`a:defRPr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#adefrpr), [`a:lstStyle`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alststyle), [`a:lvl1pPr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alvl1ppr), [`a:lvl2pPr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alvl2ppr), [`a:lvl3pPr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alvl3ppr), [`a:lvl4pPr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alvl4ppr), [`a:lvl5pPr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alvl5ppr), [`a:lvl6pPr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alvl6ppr), [`a:lvl7pPr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alvl7ppr), [`a:lvl8pPr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alvl8ppr), [`a:lvl9pPr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alvl9ppr), [`p:bodyStyle`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pbodystyle), [`p:defaultTextStyle`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pdefaulttextstyle), [`p:otherStyle`](../format/namespaces/p.md#potherstyle), [`p:titleStyle`](../format/namespaces/p.md#ptitlestyle), [`p:txStyles`](../format/namespaces/p.md#ptxstyles)

<a id="text.runs"></a>

### Runs and character formatting

`text.runs` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Size (hundredths of a point), bold, italic, all caps and small caps, single and double strikethrough, superscript and subscript (baseline %), character spacing, kerning threshold, highlight.

**Office behaviour:** Superscripts and subscripts are drawn at two thirds of the size, raised or lowered by the baseline percentage of the full size (measured). Equalized character height (normalizeH) stretches every glyph's ink from the baseline to the font's ascent (OS/2 usWinAscent; measured: all letters of 32 pt Calibri 40 px tall, descenders lifted); kumimoji keeps digits upright in vertical text. A line break's own size (a:br a:rPr) sizes its line only when the line has no other text: a 60 pt break after 20 pt text leaves the line 24 pt high, alone it makes it 72 pt (generated/text-more, slide 4). A paragraph's own default run properties (a:pPr/a:defRPr) format its runs before the list styles do (POI's bug63290: a run without properties in the paragraph's red 40 pt). altLang, bmk, dirty, err, noProof, smtClean and smtId are editing state. Verified with generated/text-basic, text-underline and text-more. A newline written in a run's text (a:t) breaks the line as a:br does but sizes it like the line's other characters (LibreOffice's n828390_2: "Linux" at 20 pt ended by a 24 pt run of a newline is a 24 pt line). Underlines and strikethroughs run on through the spaces between words, not through spaces ending a line (POI's aascu deck: 'Math Problem Solving' in one piece). A strikethrough's top lies the OS/2 strikeout position above the baseline's row of the slide's pixels, at least a pixel thick, both taken at the font's size in whole pixels and each rounded; a double one is two such lines a thickness above and below the single one's place; each run keeps its own, whatever runs beside it (measured with local/ulmix-a and -c: Aptos, Calibri, Arial and Times New Roman from 8 to 36 pt, 72 pt beside 12 pt text). A presentation without p:defaultTextStyle (as other programs write them) takes PowerPoint's own, Office's default: the theme's fonts, kerning from 12 pt and half an inch more indent at every level (LibreOffice decks with Calibri text: kerned without any kern attribute; a text box's levels 36 pt apart).

**Elements (5):** [`a:br`](../format/namespaces/a.md#abr), [`a:highlight`](../format/namespaces/a.md#ahighlight), [`a:r`](../format/namespaces/a.md#ar), [`a:rPr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#arpr), [`a:t`](../format/namespaces/a.md#at)

**Specification:** §21.1.2.2.1 Text Line Break (p.3209); §21.1.2.3.4 Highlight Color (p.3251); §21.1.2.3.8 Text Run (p.3257); §21.1.2.3.9 Text Run Properties (p.3257); §21.1.2.3.11 Text String (p.3264)
