<a id="text.fonts"></a>

### Fonts per script

`text.fonts` · P0 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Choose the latin, East Asian or complex-script font per character; resolve theme fonts; see engine.fonts.

**Office behaviour:** PowerPoint draws every missing font in Calibri, whatever its panose, pitch family or charset say (measured with serif, monospaced, script and decorative hints in generated/text-body). Some names that are no family's still find one (160 names measured against the installed fonts, in this order): a style word run on is dropped (TahomaBold: Tahoma, regular); a name beginning with Arial, Helv, Times, Courier, Segoe, Palatino or Lucida Console takes Arial, Arial, Times New Roman, Courier New, Segoe UI, Palatino Linotype or Consolas whatever follows (ArialMT, TimesNewRomanPSMT, SegoeUI); a trailing " Bold" or " Italic" sets the style (Cambria Bold); a hyphen ends the name (Georgia-Bold: Georgia, regular). The free families Liberation Sans, Liberation Serif and DejaVu Sans (also DejaVu Sans Mono) take Arial, Times New Roman and Verdana when they are not installed; Liberation Mono, DejaVu Serif, Arimo, Tinos, Carlito and Caladea do not (measured by line widths; LibreOffice's 3d_rotated_text). Other names, also PostScript names such as CambriaMath, become Calibri (generated/text-more, slide 7). Characters inserted from symbol fonts (stored at U+F000 to U+F0FF) take the run's symbol font (a:sym): Wingdings 1 to 3, Symbol and Webdings render with their own glyphs (generated/text-more). A Latin font naming the theme's East Asian font (+mn-ea, +mj-ea) where the theme has none stands for the theme's font of the East Asian script of the run's language, else of its alternative language (local/mixfonts-d: Latin letters with altLang zh-CN drawn in 等线). Text that no style names a font for is set in Arial, not in the theme's font (PowerPoint's PDF of LibreOffice's tdf165341, whose master has empty title and body styles: its title in Arial 18 pt; so are a text box under a defaultTextStyle without fonts and the levels an empty style leaves); chart text in the theme's body font. charset, panose and pitchFamily only help find substitutes; fonts are resolved by name. Verified with generated/text-basic, text-eastasian, text-rtl and text-more.

**Elements (4):** [`a:cs`](../format/namespaces/a.md#acs), [`a:ea`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aea), [`a:latin`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alatin), [`a:sym`](../format/namespaces/a.md#asym)

**Specification:** §21.1.2.3.1 Complex Script Font (p.3242); §21.1.2.3.3 East Asian Font (p.3249); §21.1.2.3.7 Latin Font (p.3255); §21.1.2.3.10 Symbol Font (p.3262)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1392, 2.1.1394, 2.1.1397, 2.1.1400

<a id="text.embeddedFonts"></a>

### Embedded fonts

`text.embeddedFonts` · P1 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Decode the font parts back to TrueType with our own Embedded OpenType and MicroType Express decoder (EOT header, LZCOMP with its adaptive Huffman trees, Compact Table Format to glyf, loca, cvt, hdmx and VDMX); lay text out with them where the family is not installed and carry them in the HTML as @font-face.

**Office behaviour:** PowerPoint stores every style of an embedded family (p:regular, p:bold, p:italic and p:boldItalic, also styles the presentation does not use) as a part of type application/x-fontdata: Embedded OpenType version 0x00020002 with the subset and compressed flags, without XOR. The subset keeps the glyph ids and holds the glyphs of the characters the presentation uses and of printable ASCII; the other glyphs are empty. A family that is installed is taken from the system. Decoded, the kept glyphs' outlines and programs, hmtx, cmap and hdmx equal the installed fonts and VDMX is byte for byte the same (14 faces of Georgia, Comic Sans MS, Consolas and Segoe Script); Chromium's font sanitizer accepts the rebuilt fonts. generated/text-fonts, converted with its embedded fonts in place of the installed ones, renders pixel for pixel like with the installed fonts (95.0 %, the usual gap of small text). A variable font is embedded as the same file for every style (seen with Wix Madefor Display, wght 400 to 800): the slide show draws bold at the Bold instance (wght 700) and kerns it from GPOS (see engine.textLayout), while Slide.Export draws such text in Arial, so slide exports of decks with embedded variable fonts are no references.

**Elements (7):** [`p:bold`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pbold), [`p:boldItalic`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pbolditalic), [`p:embeddedFont`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pembeddedfont), [`p:embeddedFontLst`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pembeddedfontlst), [`p:font`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pfont), [`p:italic`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pitalic), [`p:regular`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pregular)

**Specification:** §19.2.1.1 Bold Embedded Font (p.2533); §19.2.1.2 Bold Italic Embedded Font (p.2534); §19.2.1.9 Embedded Font (p.2538); §19.2.1.10 Embedded Font List (p.2538); §19.2.1.13 Embedded Font Name (p.2539); §19.2.1.16 Italic Embedded Font (p.2543) and 1 more

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1100, 2.1.1102

<a id="text.fillOutline"></a>

### Text fill and outline

`text.fillOutline` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

gradFill, blipFill, pattFill and a:ln inside run properties.

**Rendering:** SVG text paint or glyph outlines from our layout engine for gradient, picture and outlined text.

**Office behaviour:** Gradient and pattern fills span each line separately (from the line's start to its end and from its ascent to its descent); the outline is centered on the glyph edge and drawn over the fill. Verified with generated/text-effects.

<a id="text.underline"></a>

### Underlines

`text.underline` · P1 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** The 18 underline styles drawn by our layout engine as rectangles on the slide's pixels, with position and thickness from the font, shared by the runs side by side in a line.

**Office behaviour:** All 18 underline styles on whole pixels: the baseline rounded to its row of the slide's pixels, the post table's underline position (the line's top) and thickness taken at the font's size in whole pixels and each rounded, the thickness at least a pixel (measured with local/ulmix-a and -b: 144 lines of Aptos, Calibri, Arial and Times New Roman from 8 to 22 pt, their baselines at every fraction of a pixel, all on PowerPoint's rows); dashed styles repeat in units of that thickness (dotted 1:1, dash 3:2, long dash 7:4, dot dash 3:2:1:2, dot dot dash 3:2:1:2:1:2); a double one is two lines of half the thickness (rounded up) from the thickness above the plain one's top to its bottom, a heavy one reaches from there to half the thickness below the top, with the same pattern (measured with local/ulmix-c: Arial and Aptos from 8 to 36 pt, one to four pixels thick); wavy ones are waves four thicknesses long. Underlined runs side by side in a line share one underline, each in its own style and color: at the mean of their positions and thicknesses (in whole pixels) weighted by their widths, rounded, its dashes running on from the first run's start through the words; raised runs underline on the line's baseline with the runs beside them, without counting, lowered ones on their own (measured with local/ulmix-c and -d: 72 pt beside 12 pt text, long or short, either first, three sizes and two fonts in a line, single beside double, two colors, dotted lines, raised and lowered runs, in Arial, Calibri and Aptos). An underline line of its own (a:uLn) outlines the plain underline, which is filled with a:uFill (or the text's color): the line lies half inside and half outside the edges, at least a pixel, with its own dash (measured with Arial 40 pt and uLn of 0.25 to 6 pt: 0.25 pt adds a row of line above and below a fill of three rows, 2 pt leaves one row of fill between three rows of line, from 3 pt the line covers the fill); uLnTx and uFillTx follow the text. Measured with generated/text-underline (every style in the text color and in a color of its own, lines of their own, runs of different sizes): 76.1 -> 90.7 %, the rest one-pixel rounding of the line's position.

**Elements (4):** [`a:uFill`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aufill), [`a:uFillTx`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aufilltx), [`a:uLn`](../format/namespaces/a.md#auln), [`a:uLnTx`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aulntx)

**Specification:** §21.1.2.3.12 Underline Fill (p.3265); §21.1.2.3.13 Underline Fill Properties Follow Text (p.3265); §21.1.2.3.14 Underline Stroke (p.3266); §21.1.2.3.15 Underline Follows Text (p.3267)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1401, 2.1.1402

<a id="text.effects"></a>

### Text effects

`text.effects` · P2 · difficulty 4 · done

effectLst on runs (shadow, glow, reflection, soft edges).

**Rendering:** The shape effect filters on the glyph layer, sized with the line's largest font; scaled and skewed shadows as transformed copies about the shape's box; reflections mirrored line by line; warped WordArt text gets the same filters.

**Office behaviour:** PowerPoint sizes text effects with the largest font of each line, unlike a shape's (measured with probe decks of 8 to 240 pt lines in several fonts, in exported slides and in the slide show alike): outer shadows (distance and blur) and glow grow with (size / 78 pt)^0.695, inner shadows with size / 117 pt, a reflection's blur with size / 79 pt and its distance with size / 14.5 pt. A reflection mirrors each line about its baseline: the distance extends the mirrored box below the baseline by half the scaled distance and the fade runs over that box. Text glow is dilated by 0.46 r and blurred by r / 6 at the outer scale. Verified with generated/text-effects.

<a id="text.fields"></a>

### Fields

`text.fields` · P1 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** slidenum and datetime1–13 are computed when rendering; other field types keep their stored text.

**Elements (1):** [`a:fld`](../format/namespaces/a.md#afld)

**Specification:** §21.1.2.2.4 Text Field (p.3222)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1385

<a id="text.columns"></a>

### Text columns

`text.columns` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

a:bodyPr/@numCol and @spcCol.

**Rendering:** The text is laid out at the column width and its lines are shared out over the columns.

**Office behaviour:** A column takes the lines that fit the text area's height, but at least an even share of all lines, and the last column the rest (measured with the object model, nine boxes of 4 to 12 lines in 2 and 3 columns: 7 lines in 2 columns 5 lines high are 5 and 2, 3 lines high 4 and 3; 12 lines in 3 columns 5 lines high 5, 5 and 2, 3 lines high 4, 4 and 4; 33 lines in three columns overflowing 11, 11 and 11). So an auto-sized box keeps all its text in the first column (LibreOffice's auto-height-multi-col-shape). Verified with generated/text-body.

<a id="text.vertical"></a>

### Vertical and rotated text

`text.vertical` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

a:bodyPr/@vert (vert, vert270, eaVert, mongolianVert, wordArtVert), @upright and @rot.

**Rendering:** Rotate the line layout, or lay out top to bottom for East Asian and WordArt vertical text.

**Office behaviour:** vert and vert270 lay the text out along the shape's height and turn it by 90 or 270 degrees; the insets stay with the shape's sides and the anchor works across the turned lines (the text's top faces right for vert, left for vert270). eaVert keeps ideographs, kana and full-width forms upright (turned back about the middle of their em box) and stacks lines right to left; mongolianVert stacks them left to right. wordArtVert stacks upright characters in columns from the left (wordArtVertRtl from the right): characters and columns 1.41 x the size apart, the baseline 1.12 x the size into each cell, words that do not fit break between characters, trailing spaces take no room (measured). rot turns the laid-out text, vertical text too, about the middle of the text rectangle (LibreOffice's tdf149551: 12 degrees, 60 -> 70 %). upright keeps the text level in rotated shapes, laid out with width and height swapped between 45 and 135 (225 and 315) degrees. Table cells (tcPr vert) wrap at the row height (generated/text- vertical). anchorCtr centers turned text along the shape's height as it centers lines across a text area, also where the text is longer than the height (LibreOffice's oox/tdf151008_eaVertAnchor: columns of East Asian vertical text centered on their box, 82.2 → 94.5 %; tdf149551_vert270AndTextRot 88.5 → 97.4 %).

<a id="text.warp"></a>

### WordArt transforms

`text.warp` · P2 · difficulty 5 · done

**Rendering:** Glyph outlines from the font, bent along the warp's guide paths (41 presets) into SVG paths.

**Office behaviour:** The text's ink box fills the envelope between each pair of guide paths (glyph tops on the upper path, baselines on the lower one), measured in arc length. Lines are shared out among the envelopes in order (textButtonPour: two lines take the upper and the middle one, four lines two, one and one; empty paragraphs count), and all envelopes share one scale each way (measured with probe decks of H strokes, residuals under 0.5 px): along, the envelope whose ink is longest for the middle of its envelope (the mean of its two paths' lengths) fills it and sets the scale, the others' ink taking its part where the alignment puts it; across, the tallest ink fills its envelope and the others' is centered at that scale, a line without ink counting with its line box (a line of A's before an empty paragraph keeps 0.56 of its envelope's height) (local/warp-buttons 61.5 to 88.8 %; LibreOffice's tdf139618_WordArtBitmapFill, textDeflateInflate, 65.6 to 80.7 %). Presets with one or three paths put every glyph rigid on the path at its middle, at the glyph's size and placed by the line's alignment; the lines are shared out among the paths alike (textButton: one line on the upper arc, two on the arc and the middle line, three on all three, four with two on the upper arc; empty paragraphs count), and such text is not wrapped (a line longer than the text box stays whole on the lower arc). Text from old WordArt (fromWordArt) is scaled evenly to the path's length (measured with textArchUp in generated/text-body). Verified with generated/text-effects (WordArt transforms). Every line follows a parallel of the path, a line pitch apart, on the side away from the box's center: on a circle or an arch up the last line lies on the path and the others outside it, under an arch down the first line lies on the path and the others below. Glyphs are spaced by arc length on their line's parallel, from the parallel's start or as aligned (insets do not count), and then lowered: the line on the path has its baseline 0.79 em under the top of its line box below the path (eight fonts, 12 to 72 pt, within 0.01 em; 4.8 px under textButton's middle line in 24 pt Aptos). Other text shrinks when it is longer than PowerPoint measures the path: an arc counts its width radius times its sweep, as if it were a circle (textArchUp with eight H's: 177.6 px a radian on arches of 10 to 40 degrees with a width radius of 180 px, 91 px with one of 90 px), so that text runs on along the ellipse past the ends of an arch flatter than that circle; a line longer than its parallel is centered on it (LibreOffice's tdf116350-texteffects 69.1 to 73.5 %). Known deviations: old WordArt with several lines on part of an elliptic arc comes out larger than in PowerPoint (LibreOffice's tdf125573); on an arch down of an ellipse PowerPoint places the glyphs closer to the path's start, by a factor of its arc length that depends on the ellipse (0.98 at 360 x 267 px, 0.977 at 360 x 133, 1.007 at 180 x 267, none on a circle), and textCircle on a circle at some start angles (0.982 at the default 180 degrees and at 210, none at 181, 195, 240 and 270; at 12 and 24 pt, radii of 67 and 133 px alike), for which no rule is found. Envelopes stretch the spaces at a line's start and end with its ink, not the space a wrapped line breaks at: "HH " in textPlain or textInflate leaves 0.2 of the envelope to the space and the H's side bearing, " HH" as much at the start, "HH" fills it from ink to ink (local/warpspace, every line within 2 px; LibreOffice's oox/tdf139618_ThemeColorTransparency 64.9 → 95.7 %, sd/tdf114848's slide of waves, cans and rings 53.7 → 91.9 %). Along arches, circles and buttons text follows the ellipse from the parameter of the arc's start angle through that of its sweep: PowerPoint takes the preset's angles as the ellipse's parameter, not as the visual angles its path is drawn by; both agree where the arc's ends lie on the axes (local/archwa-*: under a textArchDown of 322.76° in a 2:1 box text starts 217° round the ellipse, not 237°; LibreOffice's sd/tdf116350-texteffects 73.5 → 84.0 %, tdf125573_FontWorkScaleX 51.5 → 66.3 %).

**Elements (1):** [`a:prstTxWarp`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aprsttxwarp)

**Specification:** §20.1.9.19 Preset Text Warp (p.2917)

<a id="text.threeD"></a>

### 3D text

`text.threeD` · P3 · difficulty 5 · done

**Rendering:** scene3d/sp3d on the text body; same approach as 3D shapes.

**Office behaviour:** A scene of the text body's own (a:bodyPr/a:scene3d, a:sp3d; Format Shape, Text Options) gives the glyphs a body while the shape stays flat: the glyph outlines are extruded, beveled, backed and contoured like a 3D shape's (extrusion in the text's color unless extrusionClr), the text itself is the front face inside the top bevel, lit like it, and the scene turns about the center of the glyphs, not of the shape (measured). A bevel wider than half a glyph stroke runs across it to a crest along its middle, every point of it stopping there at the height the profile has, no flat front face left (found by an agent in Word's pictures of 3D text: LibreOffice's extras-ooxmlexport/ TextEffects_Props3d_Ligatures_NumForm_NumSpacing 86.1 → 96.4 %, docx/local/text-3d 77.7 → 79.4 %; generated/text-3d 98.2 → 98.4 %); text filled with a gradient or a picture keeps its bevel within the strokes' mean thickness, its height in proportion (not measured yet). Text in a 3D-turned shape turns with the shape's front face; a:flatTx keeps it out of the scene, facing the viewer at the shape's place (measured). Small 3D text is drawn from its outlines too (LibreOffice's 3d_rotated_text: 18 pt text turned a quarter, 41.6 to 91.4 %). Checked with generated/text-3d (97.1 %): extruded and beveled under a legacy perspective, turned only, beveled in a shape, extruded in a color of its own, flat text. The text's own shadow and glow fall from its whole glyphs, not from the front face its bevels leave: they lie behind the body (tika's testWordArt: red text with an artDeco bevel, a light contour and a soft shadow, 66.9 → 95.3 %).

**Elements (1):** [`a:flatTx`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aflattx)

**Specification:** §20.1.5.8 No text in 3D scene (p.2841)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1273

<a id="text.math"></a>

### Equations

`text.math` · P2 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** OMML laid out with the OpenType MATH table of Cambria Math (another installed math font otherwise, the mc:Fallback picture when there is none): runs in the math alphabets (m:sty, m:scr), fractions (bar, skewed, linear, stacked), scripts and pre-scripts, radicals, n-ary operators, stretchy delimiters with separators, matrices, equation arrays, accents, bars, group characters, limits, functions, boxes, border boxes and phantoms, spaced by TeX's atom classes. An equation is an inline object of the text layout, drawn as glyph outlines under transparent selectable text.

**Office behaviour:** Measured against PowerPoint with probe decks at 18, 20 and 40 pt: display equations reach at least the math font's ascent and descent and stand MathLeading below the top of their line; display operators take the first size at least 1.5 x DisplayOperatorMinHeight tall; fractions keep their size in display equations (text style below the top level) and shrink inline; delimiters take the smallest size at least twice the content's reach from the axis less 630 units (between 613 and 645 by the sizes chosen), and a delimiter with separators sets its parts in text style; skewed fractions put the fraction slash (U+2044) in the largest size within their ink; accents keep their size over one character and grow to the largest size within a wider base; an n-ary operand follows a thin space less what the display size adds to the italic correction, at least SpaceAfterScript after limits beside it; hidden limits keep their baselines; empty arguments keep a 0.9 em placeholder; m:vertJc of a group character picks the part on the baseline (top when missing). PowerPoint's exports cut off accents stretched over several characters and narrow stretched group characters; that is not imitated. Verified with generated/math.

<a id="text.rtl"></a>

### Right-to-left and complex scripts

`text.rtl` · P2 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Bidi algorithm (UAX 9) in our layout engine, complex-script fonts, rtl paragraphs and columns.

**Office behaviour:** Measured with generated/text-rtl: paragraph levels follow pPr rtl, not the text (UAX #9 weak, neutral and implicit rules); a neutral between text of two directions takes the direction of its run's language (a:rtl, else lang) — PowerPoint keeps the space of a de-DE run 'PowerPoint ' with it in an Arabic paragraph and mirrors a bracket of a Hebrew run. Right-to-left paragraphs start on the right: left margin, indent and bullet are measured from the right edge, the bullet reads right to left ('1.' shows as '.1'), alignment l and r stay absolute; justified lines stretch their spaces. Right-to-left runs are shaped right to left (HarfBuzz mirrors brackets) and drawn whole by the browser below 30 pixels per em. Tabs in lines with right-to-left text count their width only; right- to-left tables and columns see table.structure and text.columns.

**Elements (1):** [`a:rtl`](../format/namespaces/a.md#artl)

**Specification:** §21.1.2.2.8 Right to Left Run (p.3233)

<a id="text.eastAsian"></a>

### East Asian typography

`text.eastAsian` · P2 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Kinsoku line breaking rules, East Asian line breaking and hanging punctuation, ea fonts, vertical text.

**Office behaviour:** Measured with generated/text-eastasian: with the kinsoku rules (pPr eaLnBrk, on by default) a line does not start with closing punctuation or small kana and does not end with an opening bracket; hangingPunct (on in PowerPoint's default text styles) lets 、。，． hang past the end of the line instead of pushing the character before them down (Latin punctuation never hangs: a line whose closing period would pass the end moves down with its word, external/libreoffice/sd-data/ TextFittingComparisonWithMSO_1 and _2); eaLnBrk 0 turns the rules off, lines then break between any East Asian characters, punctuation included, and nothing hangs; latinLnBrk breaks Latin words anywhere to fill a line. PowerPoint ignores the presentation's own kinsoku characters (p:kinsoku). Korean breaks at spaces.

**Elements (1):** [`p:kinsoku`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pkinsoku)

**Specification:** §19.2.1.17 Kinsoku Settings (p.2544)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1103

<a id="table.structure"></a>

### Table grid and merged cells

`table.structure` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Lay out the grid ourselves (column widths from gridCol, merged cells from gridSpan, rowSpan, hMerge, vMerge) and position the cells absolutely; an HTML table would compute its own sizes.

**Office behaviour:** Row heights (a:tr/@h) are minimums; PowerPoint grows rows to fit their text. A right-to-left table (tblPr rtl) mirrors its cells with their left and right borders, not the text in them (generated/tables). Verified with generated/tables (97.7 %); a:headers and a:header only name cells for accessibility. The table's effects (a:tblPr/a:effectLst) are cast by the cells' fills and borders, not the text; translucent fills show the shadow through (LibreOffice's table-shadow-blur); a scaled shadow is scaled about its alignment point like a shape's (tdf150020-shadow-alignment: 130 % about the center).

**Elements (8):** [`a:gridCol`](../format/namespaces/a.md#agridcol), [`a:header`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aheader), [`a:headers`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aheaders), [`a:tbl`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atbl), [`a:tblGrid`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atblgrid), [`a:tblPr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atblpr), [`a:tc`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atc), [`a:tr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atr)

<a id="table.cells"></a>

### Cell formatting

`table.cells` · P0 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Margins, anchoring, vertical text, fills and borders including diagonals; shared borders resolved like PowerPoint.

**Office behaviour:** tcPr anchorCtr centers the cell's text block; bodyPr wrap none and horzOverflow have no effect in cells, the text always wraps (measured with generated/tables). Beveled cells (a:cell3D, also from the style's tcStyle) are drawn as shapes with a top bevel seen from the front: the bevel's strips and the face lit by the cell's light rig (three-point from the top by default) and material (plastic), the fill on the face inside the bevel (generated/tables-more: circle, angle and relaxed inset bevels, faces lighter under the three-point rig). Borders are antialiased where they lie, not snapped as shape outlines are: below 1.5 px one pixel wide, else their own width, their edges moved on to the next of PowerPoint's antialiasing samples, a quarter's top down a column and an eighth's left along a row (local/lw-probe: one-cell tables with borders of 0.25 to 6 pt at quarter-pixel offsets, 76 of 88 edges exact, the rest a position PowerPoint rounds to 1/576 inch). Verified with generated/tables and tables-more.

**Elements (8):** [`a:cell3D`](../format/namespaces/a.md#acell3d), [`a:lnB`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alnb), [`a:lnBlToTr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alnbltotr), [`a:lnL`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alnl), [`a:lnR`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alnr), [`a:lnT`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alnt), [`a:lnTlToBr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alntltobr), [`a:tcPr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atcpr)

<a id="table.styles"></a>

### Table styles

`table.styles` · P0 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Combine whole table, banding, first/last row and column and corner cells in PowerPoint's order for the flags set in tblPr. PowerPoint draws only its 74 built-in styles, so we need their definitions, harvested from PowerPoint itself.

**Office behaviour:** PowerPoint draws tables only in its 74 built-in styles, looked up by a:tableStyleId; the definitions in ppt/tableStyles.xml are not used for drawing (measured with patched decks): a built-in id draws the built-in style whatever the file defines under it (Apache POI's table-with-different-font-colors, 93.1 to 98.6 %), and a style of the file's own, even an exact copy of a built-in one under another id, or an inline a:tableStyle draws No Style, Table Grid (POI's bug68703, a Google Slides style, 61.2 to 97.5 %). The 74 styles as PowerPoint writes them into ppt/tableStyles.xml are kept as a resource (generated/tables-more slide 2: six styles with an empty style list, 98.4 %). Verified with generated/tables and tables-more. A table without tableStyleId is drawn in No Style, Table Grid, not in the presentation's default style (measured with a patched deck; Apache POI's table-with-no-theme).

**Elements (30):** [`a:band1H`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aband1h), [`a:band1V`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aband1v), [`a:band2H`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aband2h), [`a:band2V`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aband2v), [`a:bottom`](../format/namespaces/a.md#abottom), [`a:fill`](../format/namespaces/a.md#afill), [`a:firstCol`](../format/namespaces/a.md#afirstcol), [`a:firstRow`](../format/namespaces/a.md#afirstrow), [`a:insideH`](../format/namespaces/a.md#ainsideh), [`a:insideV`](../format/namespaces/a.md#ainsidev), [`a:lastCol`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alastcol), [`a:lastRow`](../format/namespaces/a.md#alastrow), [`a:left`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aleft), [`a:neCell`](../format/namespaces/a.md#anecell), [`a:nwCell`](../format/namespaces/a.md#anwcell), [`a:right`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aright), [`a:seCell`](../format/namespaces/a.md#asecell), [`a:swCell`](../format/namespaces/a.md#aswcell), [`a:tableStyle`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atablestyle), [`a:tableStyleId`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atablestyleid), [`a:tblBg`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atblbg), [`a:tblStyle`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atblstyle), [`a:tblStyleLst`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atblstylelst), [`a:tcBdr`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atcbdr), [`a:tcStyle`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atcstyle), [`a:tcTxStyle`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atctxstyle), [`a:tl2br`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atl2br), [`a:top`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atop), [`a:tr2bl`](../format/namespaces/a.md#atr2bl), [`a:wholeTbl`](../format/namespaces/a.md#awholetbl)

<a id="chart.part"></a>

### Chart part and settings

`chart.part` · P1 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Read chart settings and render from cached data; print settings are ignored.

**Office behaviour:** A chart's c:clrMapOvr replaces the slide's color mapping for the chart, text included (generated/colormap). The chart area's corners are rounded unless c:roundedCorners says otherwise; PowerPoint rounds them where the element is missing too, by 9.8 pt whatever the chart's size (measured with local/rounded: 200 x 150 to 300 x 450 pt). Blank cells (c:dispBlanksAs) show as gaps where the element is missing, as zero where it says so or names nothing, or spanned by the line without a marker (generated/charts-data). Labels of values beyond the axis are hidden as PowerPoint writes c:showDLblsOverMax 0 (8 and 11 on an axis up to 6 go unlabelled). Dates of the 1904 system (c:date1904) are moved to the 1900 one. Print settings (c:printSettings, header and footer, page setup) and editing state (c:protection, c:userInterface, c:externalData) do not change the picture. Verified with generated/charts, charts-more, charts-details and charts-data.

<a id="chart.barColumn"></a>

### Bar and column charts

`chart.barColumn` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Clustered, stacked and 100 % stacked bars as SVG rectangles.

**Office behaviour:** Negative bars of a series that inverts them (c:invertIfNegative) take the fill of c14:invertSolidFillFmt, else white, with a black hairline where the series has no line of its own (measured with generated/charts-details). Verified with generated/charts, charts-details and charts- data.

<a id="chart.pie"></a>

### Pie and doughnut charts

`chart.pie` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Arcs as SVG paths; pie-of-pie and bar-of-pie with series lines.

**Office behaviour:** Pie of pie and bar of pie (c:ofPieChart): the split (splitType pos with splitPos, 2 by default; val, percent, cust with c:secondPiePt) moves points to a second pie or a stacked bar beside the main pie, which shows them as one slice formatted by the extra data point and centered towards the second plot; the second plot is secondPieSize % of the main pie high, a bar half as wide as high; the gap at gapWidth 100 is 0.514 (pie) or 0.259 (bar) of the main pie's diameter; the second pie's slices start (clockwise) where the combined slice ends, the bar's first point on top; series lines (c:serLines) join the combined slice's edges to the second plot's top and bottom. Measured with generated/charts- details: 0.06 -> 0.97 on that slide. Verified with generated/charts, charts- details and local/pielabels. A fill of the series' own colors the points without one even where the colors vary by point (LibreOffice's tdf150176 and tdf146756: the first slice in bg2). The room for outside labels follows the series' label position, not single points' (centered labels with some at bestFit keep the pie at full size: tdf125444, percentage-number-formats); with them the pie is 0.876 of half the plot's smaller side less 0.163 of the labels' size across (Word's PDFs of LibreOffice's extras-layout/tdf137116: 53.71 pt of 63.0 at 10 pt, outside_long_data_label 59.0 of 69.0, tdf134866 99.25 of 115; local/pielabels slide 4: 142.5 of 163.5; tdf137116 79.2 → 94.3 %, outside_long_data_label 81.9 → 99.6 %, pielabels 98.9 → 99.2 %). Word keeps that room wherever a series has data labels at all, even ones that show nothing (c:dLbls of every show* 0; scratch/WordProbes/probe_pielabels.py on LibreOffice's extras-ooxmlexport/tdf123873: 64.7 pt in radius with the flags alone or with text properties, a separator or point entries, as with values shown, 75.5 pt without c:dLbls; tdf123873 84.4 → 97.0 %). A plot area placed by hand around the pie (layoutTarget inner) holds the pie with its series' explosion; single exploded slices reach beyond it (tdf146756_bestFit, tdf146487).

<a id="chart.scatterBubble"></a>

### Scatter and bubble charts

`chart.scatterBubble` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Value axes on both sides; bubble size by area or width.

**Office behaviour:** The largest bubble takes 0.3 s of the length it measures against less itself, D = 0.3 s (L - D), s = bubbleScale / 100 and L the smaller side of the plot area before the axis labels plus 11.49 pt (at 100 % 0.2307 m + 2.65 pt, measured through COM; Word's docx/local/bubble-* 28.17, 49.82, 67.01 and 80.96 pt at 50, 100, 150 and 200 %, within 0.003 pt); sizes by area or width. Every drawn bubble's value grows into a span of 0.1824 s / (1 + 0.3 s) of the final axis range on either side, whatever its own size, and the axes take their automatic bounds of that without the 5 % headroom, until they settle (Word's 29 bubble-* and 9 bax-* probes, x and y, of other chart and plot sizes; PowerPoint's charts-more and charts-details alike); bubble legend keys are round. bubble3D is drawn flat.

**Elements (9):** [`c15:bubble3D`](../format/namespaces/c15.md#c15bubble3d), [`c16:bubble3D`](../format/namespaces/c16.md#c16bubble3d), [`c:bubble3D`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cbubble3d), [`c:bubbleChart`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cbubblechart), [`c:bubbleScale`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cbubblescale), [`c:scatterChart`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cscatterchart), [`c:scatterStyle`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cscatterstyle), [`c:showNegBubbles`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cshownegbubbles), [`c:sizeRepresents`](../format/namespaces/c.md#csizerepresents)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1440, 2.1.1441, 2.1.1534

<a id="chart.radar"></a>

### Radar charts

`chart.radar` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Polar axes as SVG.

**Office behaviour:** One spoke per category clockwise from 12 o'clock; the value gridlines are polygons, the value axis line is drawn as every spoke and its labels stand along the first spoke (1.14 s - 1.25 pt left of it); the category axis draws neither line nor gridlines. The radar is centered in the plot area with radius min(W/2 - (widest label + 0.3056 s - 3.005), H/2 - (L + 0.3127 s - 5.248)), measured through COM with 25 layouts. Category labels stand 6 pt beyond their spoke, left- or right-aligned at the sides and centered above and below. The value axis ends at the first major unit from the largest value, without the 5 % headroom of other charts (measured: 7.9 on 0 … 8, 9.6 on 0 … 10); charts without style part draw spokes only where the axis has a line of its own. The category axis' major gridlines also make the spokes, but in the value axis' line, not in their own (local/radarspk-*: red gridlines draw gray spokes, a blue value axis line blue ones; poi/radar-chart 85.9 → 89.5 %).

**Elements (2):** [`c:radarChart`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cradarchart), [`c:radarStyle`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cradarstyle)

**Specification:** §21.2.2.153 Radar Charts (p.3430); §21.2.2.154 Radar Style (p.3430)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1530, 2.1.1531

<a id="chart.stock"></a>

### Stock charts

`chart.stock` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** High-low lines and up/down bars over line series.

**Office behaviour:** High-low lines join each category's lowest and highest point, up/down bars run from the first to the last series (category width / (1 + gap width)) and cover the lines; the series lines are usually invisible. A dot marker is a circle half the marker size. Legend entries of series without a line keep the narrow square-key width and show only their marker.

**Elements (1):** [`c:stockChart`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cstockchart)

**Specification:** §21.2.2.198 Stock Charts (p.3444)

<a id="chart.surface"></a>

### Surface charts

`chart.surface` · P3 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Contour bands in 2D; 3D surfaces with chart.threeD.

**Office behaviour:** Contour (top view) charts: the band scale is the value axis without headroom; the surface is a top view of a 3D box whose sides are proportional to the category and series counts, 97.3 % of the inner plot area. Each grid cell splits along the diagonal with the larger value sum; facets are lit with 0.52 + 0.595 n·l (light from the upper right at 31 degrees; facets turned away keep darkening) plus a specular highlight; the top view's box is 0.6 × its base high, scaled by the inner plot area's height over its width (measured with tilted planes in square, wide and tall charts, local/contour: within 2 levels). 3D surfaces (meshes cut into the value bands, drawn back to front by their grid cells) and wireframes (band-colored grid and contour lines) are drawn with chart.threeD. Contour charts verified with generated/charts-more; 3D surfaces see chart.threeD.

**Elements (5):** [`c:bandFmt`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cbandfmt), [`c:bandFmts`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cbandfmts), [`c:surface3DChart`](../format/namespaces/c.md#csurface3dchart), [`c:surfaceChart`](../format/namespaces/c.md#csurfacechart), [`c:wireframe`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cwireframe)

**Specification:** §21.2.2.13 Band Format (p.3385); §21.2.2.14 Band Formats (p.3385); §21.2.2.203 3D Surface Charts (p.3445); §21.2.2.204 Surface Charts (p.3445); §21.2.2.230 Wireframe (p.3451)

**MS-OI29500:** 2.1.1436, 2.1.1556, 2.1.1557

<a id="chart.axes"></a>

### Axes, gridlines and number formats

`chart.axes` · P1 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Reproduce Office's automatic minimum, maximum and major unit; number formats need an Excel number-format engine; date axes, logarithmic and reversed scales.

**Office behaviour:** Measured: zero is included unless all values lie in the top sixth of their maximum; the maximum gets 5 % headroom; with a fixed maximum and an automatic minimum the ticks count down from the maximum (700000 over data down to -479325: -700000 … 700000); the major unit is the smallest 1-2-5 step giving at most ten intervals: on a vertical axis as many as leave room for two labels more in the plot area's outer box (the chart less its title, legend and 11 pt edges, before the axis labels, less 0.2 pt), each label a single line of its font high (measured in Word, docx chart.axes; local/catwrap-horizontal 95.6 → 96.5 %), in a plot area placed by hand each at least 1.1 line heights tall; 100 % stacked axes are exactly 0 to 100 %. Built-in format 14 ("m/d/yyyy") shows the locale's short date. Numbers and dates take the system's regional format, not the chart's language (c:lang; measured: an en-US chart shows 2,5 on a German system); the converter takes it as an option, the current culture by default. Category labels that do not fit their slots wrap at the slot's width where every word fits it, else all rotate by -45 degrees (local/catwrap-horizontal: two- to six-word labels wrap, also in a chart 200 pt short; one word wider than its slot turns all labels); date axes that label as many dates as fit show all of them, wrapped, where their words fit (LibreOffice's bnc889755), and built-in format 17 (mmm-yy) reads "Okt 12" on a German system. Rotated labels sit with the end of their middle line at the category; in Word's charts labels turned up by -45 degrees are boxes whose top right corner sits on the category, the first category their width across from the outer edge, the last their line's height up from the right one (agent AK in Word's PDFs: powertools/CU004-Chart-Cached-Data-04 87.8 → 98.0 %, libreoffice/extras-ooxmlimport/tdf82824 90.1 → 98.8 %); labels of an axis crossing inside the plot area stay at the axis. Display units (c:dispUnits: built-in or custom) divide the value labels, and their label (c:dispUnitsLbl) shows its own text or the unit's name in the chart's language (German Office: Millionen, x 10000), turned a quarter beside the labels with its end at the top of the plot area, in a column of its own. Category axes label every tickLblSkip-th category and mark every tickMarkSkip- th, their labels keep lblOffset % of the default distance; numeric categories (dates on a date axis) take the axis's number format, months abbreviated as Office does (German: Mrz, Jun, Sep). A plot area placed by hand (c:manualLayout) still turns category labels that do not fit by -45 degrees. Horizontal value axes divide the plot area's width before the labels beside it take their room into intervals of at least four font sizes, however wide their labels are, which then overlap (measured: bar and scatter charts 150 to 900 pt wide with 9 and 12 pt labels; Office 2016 charts keep one label wide plus three font sizes). Category labels beside a vertical axis (bar charts) wrap at half the outer plot area's width less half the first and half the last value label, keep the lines that fit their category's height with an ellipsis after the last (centered without it), and stand as blocks as wide as their longest line beside the axis, their lines centered (measured: bar charts 200 to 950 pt wide with 9 to 24 pt labels and value labels of several widths or none; local probes). In Word category labels of a horizontal axis turned a quarter by their own rotation (bodyPr rot ±5400000) wrap at half the outer plot area's height, their lines centered on each other, the block's middle at the category (docx/local/tlb-*, tlc-*: a Calibri 9 label beside charts 150 to 550 pt high and 288 or 432 pt wide breaks where a line would pass half the height less 10.1 to 12.7 pt; LibreOffice's extras-layout/xaxis-labelbreak 22.8 → 92.6 %). A plot area placed by hand wraps them at half the chart's width less those overhangs and keeps all labels inside the chart: it moves left until the value label at its right edge is 6 pt inside, then gives up width on the left to the labels beside it (LibreOffice's tdf48041: 60.8 to 90.2 %). Not yet: the plot area of narrow bar charts whose labels nearly fill their categories starts lower in PowerPoint. Multi-level categories (c:multiLvlStrRef) put every outer level in a row of its own, the label gap below the categories, each name centered over its group with lines along the group edges from the axis through the row; with c:noMultiLvlLbl the names join their group's first label as further lines; beside a vertical axis on the left the outer levels take columns of their own, the names turned (measured in Word, docx chart.axes). Labels may hold line breaks and align in their slot (c:lblAlgn l, r: flush with its edge). Date axes (c:dateAx) count days, months or years from the first date to the last (c:baseTimeUnit, else by the closest dates), every point in the slot of its date, empty units keeping their place, labels every major unit (c:majorUnit in c:majorTimeUnit) or as many as fit (generated/charts-data: 1, 4, 5, 12 January on a day axis, 1 and 15 January sharing January). Days without a major unit whose dates do not fit label every day, every second or every seventh, the first that fits, flat, turned by 45° (slots of 1.73 line heights) or, for days and second days, by 90° (1.18 line heights), else the first of every month, the slots taken on the plot area's width for flat labels (local/da-*: 20 to 200 days on 181 to 829 pt axes, the same in Word; docx/external/powertools/CU004 57.7 → 87.8 %, libreoffice/extras-ooxmlimport/tdf82824 49.8 → 90.0 %). Known deviation: below labels turned by 45° PowerPoint's plot area is up to 5 pt narrower on each side than ours. Lines and areas run on through the dates without data between their points (dispBlanksAs only concerns blank cells). A plot area placed with its labels (layoutTarget outer) holds them all: half a value label at the top, the first turned category label at the left edge. Category labels stand (1 + lblOffset / 100) / 2 default gaps below the axis (measured with offsets of 0, 200, 400 and 1000). Display unit labels stand 3.6 pt beside the value labels in a column 9.5 pt wider than their line, their end 2.5 pt below the top of the plot area. Horizontal value axes keep each interval one label and three font sizes wide (a 418 pt scatter axis up to 9 steps by 2). Checked with generated/charts-data, charts-details (99.2 %) and a label offset probe (99.6 %). The sides of category and value axes follow the chart type whatever c:axPos says: a bar chart's category axis is vertical (LibreOffice's tdf48041 saves it at b, PowerPoint shows the categories on the left). A value axis deleted beside another of its direction takes that one's scale, which spans the series of both; where both are deleted they take the first's; both shown, each keeps its own (measured in Word with docx/local/csx-*: columns on the primary axes, a line on secondary ones: with the secondary value axis deleted the primary one runs to 4500 for line values up to 3861, with the primary deleted the secondary one to 2500 for columns up to 1925; LibreOffice's extras-layout/tdf138773 78.6 → 83.1 %). A series has as many categories as values, more cached category names unseen (csx-*: a line of 13 values with 15 cached categories at the 13 places of the columns). A label shown on every n-th category only (c:tickLblSkip) has n slots before the labels turn, and the first and the last labelled of categories between ticks stay within the outer plot area as midCat labels do (csx-orig: "2000-01" and "2012-13f" over 13 slots of 20.3 pt labelled every second one flat, each 10 pt inside the chart, 16.5 → 89.8 %). Number formats with conditions ([<>0], [>=100]) give the numbers that meet them their section, the others the next (csx-val: [<>0]General;\ "2011-12 $/t" labels 0 "2011-12 $/t", 33.6 → 70.7 %). Without c:majorTickMark an axis has its major ticks outside, without c:minorTickMark no minor ticks; with the element, val defaults to cross (local/ticks-*). Number formats color their labels with [Red], [Blue] … or [Color1] to [Color56] (the default palette, indexed colors 8-63) in the section that formats the value (tdf137691_dataTable: negative values red).

<a id="chart.labels"></a>

### Data labels

`chart.labels` · P1 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Label text from values, categories, series names, percentages or cell ranges; automatic placement and leader lines.

**Office behaviour:** Data labels wrap at a fifth of the chart's width, box, insets and legend key included; a word longer than a line breaks between characters (measured: 'Wwwwwwwwww; 1' reads 'Wwwwwwwww' and 'w; 1'). The default separator is a line break between a percentage and a name shown without the value, else the list separator of the chart's language and a space ('One; 40' with a German Office). Pie labels, measured against PowerPoint's label boxes for pies turned in 5 degree steps (local/pieangles, local/piebestfit, local/pielabels): ctr at half the radius; inEnd centered on the middle ray where the ray leaves the label 3.7 % of the radius inside the edge; outEnd with the inner edges 4 % (vertically) and 5.1 % (horizontally) of the radius outside the edge point, straddling the ray within 18 degrees of the vertical by (1 - 3.05 tan phi) / 2 of the width and within 45 degrees of the horizontal by (1 - cot phi) / 2 of the height; bestFit inside with the outermost corner 0.9 % of the radius inside the edge where the text fits into its slice (a one-line label may break into two lines), else outside. PowerPoint's object model reports best fit labels at their outside places and the pie keeps room for outside labels (outEnd, bestFit) but not for inEnd and ctr. Moved labels (c:manualLayout x, y) are offset from their outside place by fractions of the chart and stay inside it; a moved label clear of the pie gets a leader line from the slice's edge point, straight up or down to a label above or below it, else ending in a 5.6 pt level stroke at the label's first baseline. Legend keys (c:showLegendKey) are squares of the point's fill as in legends, the text 1.108 line heights after the inset. Bubble sizes (c:showBubbleSize) label bubbles on the right, 5.5 pt off the edge. A point label's own text (c:tx: rich text, or a cell) replaces the parts, its runs keeping their own formatting (generated/charts-details slide 6: 99.6 %); its fields (a:fld VALUE, CATEGORYNAME, SERIESNAME, PERCENTAGE, XVALUE, YVALUE, BUBBLESIZE, CELLRANGE) show the point's current values whatever text they saved ('[PERCENTAGE]' in LibreOffice's tdf125444). A label's own width (c15:layout w, a fraction of the chart) replaces the fifth it wraps at (tdf146487). Text from cells (c15:datalabelsRange) leads the parts. Best fit labels inside slices narrower than a half move sideways off the middle ray until their lines keep the same distance from both of the slice's radii, then out as far as their corners stay within reach (wide labels of slices turned in 5° steps move towards the horizontal by up to 34 px, matched within 2 px; local/piebestfit 92.4 %); one-line labels near the wrap width sit up to 4 % of the radius further in in PowerPoint.

<a id="chart.titleLegend"></a>

### Titles, legend and layout

`chart.titleLegend` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Automatic layout of title, legend and plot area like Office; manual layouts are fractions of the chart area.

**Office behaviour:** Measured against COM positions (plot area, legend entries, titles) for 35 charts: title and legend sit 6 pt from the edge, the plot area keeps 11 pt from edges, title and legend; axis labels may reach 4.5 pt into that margin (1.5 pt gap to an axis title). Horizontal legends are 1.2 times as wide as their entries ([key/2][key][key/2][text]) with equal gaps, squeeze to the chart width minus 30 pt and then wrap into balanced rows; rows are one line height plus 5.85 pt. Pies with data labels shrink by 1.785 × label size. A manual plot area layout places the inner plot area (layoutTarget inner) or, by default, the plot area with its axis labels (outer); w and h are sizes (wMode, hMode factor) or far edges (edge). Verified with generated/charts, charts-details and charts-data. Axis titles of 3D charts take their room at their axis's edge and stand centered on the plot area as on flat charts, white in the black chart area of an Office 2007 style's highest level (docx/external/powertools/CU001: "Vehicles" and "Quarter" beside a 3D bar chart). Axis titles wrap at 0.8 of the chart's width; in Word an unturned title of a vertical axis at 0.2 of it, its padding included (docx/local/atl-*: Calibri 10 titles beside charts 288 to 576 pt wide break where a line would pass 0.2 of the width less 6 pt; docx/external/libreoffice/extras-layout/tdf134146). A title without text names the chart after its single series, after none without a name: the default title (docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/chart-size: Word's "Diagrammtitel", 89.8 → 97.3 %; strict 98.9 → 99.5 %, extras-layout/tdf137116 76.9 → 79.2 %). Line keys keep their size at every font size (legends of 7 to 24 pt): a 12 pt key box, the line 19.1 pt long about 12.79 pt from the entry's edge, the text at 24.31 pt; square keys scale with the line height. A legend at the side given a size of its own (c:manualLayout w, h) wraps its entries at its width less 4 pt and the key's room and shows k = min(n, ceil(height / tallest entry)) entries in equal slots of height / k, the tallest centered in its slot, all centered across (measured through COM, local/legend-manualsize; LibreOffice's tdf122765 shows 7 of 9). Keys draw markers at most half the font size and lines and marker edges at most 0.375 times it wide (local/legacy-legend* decks). A dashed line key makes every key longer: the line from 3.05 to 29.31 pt, the text at 31.5 pt (local/legacy-longkey). In Word a title without c:overlay overlays the plot as with val 1 (scratch/ WordProbes/probe_charttitle.py on LibreOffice's extras-ooxmlexport/fdo78474: the pie centred in the whole chart, below the title with val 0; fdo78474 82.2 → 99.6 %, fdo76098 88.9 → 99.8 %). PowerPoint lays the plot area out as Word does: a title or legend placed by hand stands where its layout says, but the automatic plot area keeps the room of the automatic title and of the automatic legend at its legendPos (local/pielay-*: LibreOffice's chart2/tdf122765, a pie with a moved title and a legend placed across it, has the same plot area with both moved or not; 57.3 → 98.1 %).

<a id="chart.dataTable"></a>

### Data tables

`chart.dataTable` · P3 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Table of the series values below the plot area.

**Office behaviour:** The table reaches 6 pt past the plot area's left and bottom edges; rows are L + 0.0925 s + 2.4 pt high and the name column holds the widest series name plus 1.0975 s + 2.89 pt (key 0.45 L at 0.26 L, text at 0.958 L); the plot area's left edge is the larger of the name column and the value labels, its bottom the table's top. Category labels move into the header row. The name column is at most a third of the chart's width: longer names break between words, the further lines centered under the widest, the key beside the first, and every further line makes the row a line taller with its values centered; header names break in their column less 2.7 pt on each side (local/dtable, dtable2: 99.8 and 99.3 %). Without keys the names keep 3.91 pt from the plot area. A value axis title on the left shares its room with the name column: table and plot stand where they stand without it (Word, docx/local/d3t-flat: 0.3 pt). Below 3D columns the table hangs from the floor's front edge, its columns dividing that edge, its room taken as on flat charts (docx/external/powertools/CU008, CU013: 58.6 and 62.0 → 91.5 and 92.3 %, about 5 pt wider than Word's). A deleted axis keeps its title (CU008, CU013). Line series have the legend's line keys, of a fixed size, and the name column is then wider by their text's offset less a square key's, about 1.025 s: 11.9 pt at 12 pt, 5.9 pt at 18 pt, with or without markers (local/dtkey-*); a table without c:spPr draws its borders as the style's axis lines (#898989; LibreOffice's chart2/tdf137691_dataTable 72.1 → 91.3 %).

**Elements (5):** [`c:dTable`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cdtable), [`c:showHorzBorder`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cshowhorzborder), [`c:showKeys`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cshowkeys), [`c:showOutline`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cshowoutline), [`c:showVertBorder`](../format/namespaces/c.md#cshowvertborder)

<a id="chart.formatting"></a>

### Chart formatting, chart styles and colors

`chart.formatting` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** spPr and txPr of every chart element use the shared DrawingML fill, line, effect and text code; the chart style and color parts (cs) only matter where a property is not set directly. Charts without a style part are drawn in the look of their Office 2007 style (c:style, c14:style) from the theme's fill, line and effect styles, measured in PowerPoint; series effects (a:effectLst, the styles' effect styles) are SVG filters on bars, slices, areas, bubbles and markers.

**Office behaviour:** PowerPoint draws bars on whole device pixels and thin lines and line chart vertices on the pixel grid (odd widths centered in a pixel); the renderer snaps the same way in slide pixel coordinates. Word sets bars on its 600 dpi grid of the page, their base on the axis where it is (LibreOffice's extras-layout/tdf138773: bars from 102.72 to 108.6 pt, 5.88 and 6.0 pt wide; with the measurement's rectangles in one form, 88.6 → 98.9 %, chart 95.8 → 99.5 %, CU006 96.4 → 99.8 %). The chart's areas (plot and chart area, legend, title) run from their bottom left corner right, up, left and down, where a dashed outline begins (Word's PDF of docx/external/libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/ Chart_Plot_BorderLine_Style 77.5 → 99.6 %); bars do not (Chart_BorderLine_Style). Picture fills come from the chart part's relationships; c:pictureOptions stacks them from the bar's base at their own proportions (stack) or one per c:pictureStackUnit of the value axis (stackScale), else stretches them over the bar (generated/charts-data). Verified with generated/charts, charts- more and charts-data. What a chart does not format itself shows one of the 48 Office 2007 styles (c:style, 2 without; measured with all styles, 1 to 14 series and themes whose fill, line and effect styles can be told apart), also where the chart has chart style and color parts: PowerPoint draws by them only for a title it makes up from the series' name (no c:title: the style part's 18.62 pt regular gray, generated/charts), and for Office 2016 charts (the same look and colors with and without them, local/legacy-keepstyle-column; a plot area without spPr dark in style 47, LibreOffice's tdf136754). A chart's theme override brings its own fonts (POI's aascu deck: Calibri charts in a Lucida Sans deck): style N has the color variant (N − 1) % 8 (grays, the accents in turn, one accent) and the level (N − 1) / 8. Series colors cycle through the six grays (tx1 tinted 88, 55, 78, 92, 70, 30 %) or accents with c = n / 6 + 1 cycles (one-accent styles: one per series); cycle j is shaded (before the middle) or tinted (after it) by 1.4 / (c + 1) per step in linear light, so six series are already darker. Points take fill style 1 (levels 0, 1, 4) or 3 (2, 3, 5) in their color, edges line style 1 in white (level 1) or shaded 50 % (level 4), effects effect style 2 (level 2) or 3 (3, 5). Series lines are line style 1's width times 3, 5 or 7 (level 3), markers floor(2 w + 3) pt (w the series line's own width or the style's) with an edge of line style 1 in the series color. Axis and grid lines are line style 1 in tx1 tinted 75 %; the chart area is unfilled up to level 3 (measured on a yellow slide), level 4 fills it white with a frame and tints the plot area 20 %, level 5 has a black chart area, a 95 % tx1 plot area and white text (white titles where their paragraphs have a:pPr). Text is 10 pt; titles are bold, 1.2 times the chart's text size (18 pt without one), title paragraphs without a:pPr 18 pt regular whatever the size; in Word title paragraphs whose a:pPr has a:defRPr without a size take 10 pt (probe_charttitle.py: fdo78474's "Sales" 9.96 pt bold, 18 pt without the a:pPr; fdo76098 alike). The full look needs c14:style (Office 2010) or AppVersion 12 (Office 2007); otherwise (Office 2013 and later, other programs) PowerPoint draws axis and grid lines black, level 4 plot areas in the full accent and dark gray edges black (local/legacy-* decks).

<a id="chart.threeD"></a>

### 3D charts

`chart.threeD` · P2 · difficulty 5 · done

**Rendering:** Projected 3D geometry with simple shading.

**Office behaviour:** 3D charts get a box (category axis along its width, values along its height, series or the bars' row in its depth) with the back wall, side wall and floor on its far sides, gridlines over the walls and labels at the front edges (category labels turn by 45° when crowded, dates when a label and a font size do not fit, where flat charts thin them out; series labels thin out). With right-angle axes (the default of 3D columns and bars) the box is drawn in an oblique projection: front faces stay rectangles and the depth runs along (sin rotY, sin rotX) at full length. Clustered and stacked bars stand in one row depthPercent × (1 + gapDepth) bar widths deep, each bar depthPercent × its width deep and centered; standard charts, areas, lines and surfaces give every series a row of depthPercent × the category width. Without hPercent the box is as tall as the plot area's height/width ratio times its width (times floor((categories + series)/2) category widths with series rows; areas whose categories sit on the box's edges keep the height of n − 1 of their n category widths, their depth stays depthPercent of W/n: measured on 3D stacked areas of 5 and 8 categories), fitted to the plot area less the labels, which 3D charts compute like flat ones: labels centered on the edges of area and surface charts stay within the outer edges and the series axis keeps a category label gap (measured on single boxes with colored walls at 20 rotations and 22 category/series combinations, and with local/area3d, area3d3 and inset3d: 99.4 to 99.7 %). Without right-angle axes a perspective camera looks along the plot area's centre with a field of view of perspective/2 degrees spanning its height, the scene turned by rotY then rotX, and the box is fitted each way on its own: the camera's place across is where the box centred across fills the plot area's width, its place up and down where the box centred that way fills the height, and it backs off along its axis to 1.032 times the farther of both distances, keeping both places, so the box is off centre the way it does not fill (rotY 40°: 28 px left of centre, 60°: 77 px on a 1037 px wide plot area). The automatic plot area of 3D charts lies 11 pt inside the chart on every side (COM reports 7 pt left and top); a manual inner layout places the box's plot area itself. Measured with local/persp5, persp6m and persp7 (40 empty boxes with colored walls, standard and clustered, rotY 10–80°, rotX 5–45°, perspective 20–100, depth 40–400 %, seven plot areas): corners within 1 to 5 px, the decks at 98.5 to 99.5 %. With right-angle axes the box is fitted into the plot area less 1.72 pt on the left, 2.79 above, 3.34 on the right and 1.9 below, whatever the chart's size, and the value axis takes its steps from the box as large as the whole plot area; bars lie on their side, the floor standing at the category axis on the left and the side wall below them, each lit as the face it is; labels at the floor's ends (a value axis of bars) keep their outer halves in the plot area (measured with docx/local/c3o-*: bars and columns of 432 by 252 and 300 by 300 pt, rotations 0 to 40°, depths 100 and 200 %, gap depths 0 and 150 %, gap widths 150 and 300 %, the box within 0.3 pt, turned 30 and 40° within 1.5 pt; docx/external/powertools/CU007 83.0 → 93.7 %, CU012 79.3 → 90.4 %, CU001 86.2 → 90.5 %, libreoffice/extras-ooxmlexport/chart-dupe 67.8 → 91.2 %, chart-prop 79.8 → 95.5 %; external/libreoffice/chart2/bnc864396 83.4 → 95.9 %, generated/charts-3d 94.0 → 96.8 %, local/labels3d 89.4 → 93.4 %). One light shades everything (0.4 ambient plus n · (0.239, 0.357, −0.6)): fronts 1.0, tops and the floor 0.757, right sides and the side wall 0.639, sloped area and line tops in between; cylinders and cones get measured gradients (1.07 − 0.47 t², 1.13 − 0.48 t²), pyramids flat faces, cones and pyramids taper to the bar's end, the stack's end or the axis maximum (…ToMax). Areas are slabs, lines ribbons, surfaces meshes cut into the value bands (wireframes as band-colored grid and contour lines); 3D value axes get no headroom, horizontal ones keep four font sizes per interval as on flat charts (docx/external/powertools/CU012: 0 to 5 by 0.5). 3D pies are disks 0.24 of their radius thick, slices clockwise from the back at rotY, lit by a view light (tops 0.4 + 0.43 (cos rotX + sin rotX) with a small highlight, sides a gradient from the front's light, cut faces of exploded slices lit from the right); exploded pies fit as a disk as wide as their farthest slice; in Word slices that cast a shadow (an outer shadow of their own, their series' or their style's) also fit a disc 1.0896 times as wide at the pie's bottom face, whatever the shadow (docx/local/p3x-*, p3s-*; libreoffice/writerfilter-dmapper/chart-zorder 87.7 → 98.3 %). Data labels: on bars beyond the end face, centered on the front face (ctr, inEnd, inBase on it), on areas halfway up their band on the front, beside the points of lines, on pies inside the top as far out as they fit (outEnd beyond the rim), a labelled pie fitting at 89 % of its size; horizontal value axes keep a label and three font sizes per interval. Thick walls (c:thickness, a percentage of the box's smaller side: 10 % of a 0.82 high box reach 0.083 of its width) are slabs beside, below and behind the box that take their room in the fit, carry the labels on their outer faces and the gridlines across their front and end faces (generated/charts-data slide 6: 94.8 %, 57.7 % before). Bars without fill and line draw nothing, their faces unshaded. Checked with generated/charts-3d (92.8 %, 18 slides; 88 % before the perspective fit) and local/persp to persp4 (97.0 to 99.5 %). 3D surfaces (measured with local/surf3d, surf3d2, surfbox and surfwalls: single cells of known slope, folded cells, 16 grids and walls seen from 13 sides, 93.7 to 98.5 %): the box is ⌊(categories + series − 1)/2⌋ category widths high, each cell folds along the diagonal with the larger sum, facets are lit 0.821 + 0.313 n·(0.768, 0.182, −0.614) for the normal as the camera sees it in perspective and 0.641 + 0.380 n·(0.616, 0.491, −0.616) in the box with right-angle axes, their walls 0.4 + 0.75 n·(0.572, 0.586, −0.574) for the inner normal (seen by the camera, or in the box with right-angle axes); at rotY 0 the side wall stands on the left. Views from other sides (local/backview, rotY 100° to 300°, 95.1 %): seen from behind (the camera beyond the middle of the box's depth) the back wall stands at the near end of the data and the floor's labels run along its near edge; the vertical axis always stands at the box's left end (x = 0), on whichever of its two edges lies outside on the screen, from behind on the right where its labels take no room; labels along a floor edge within 34° of the horizontal stand under it, else beside it away from the box (categories under at 27° and 32°, beside at 36°; series under at 12°, beside at 41°). Wireframes (local/wire and wire2, single cells with bands every 2): grid pieces in their band's line, a level piece on a band boundary in the band below, contour lines in the line of the band below (generated/charts-3d slide 13: 87.7 %, 76.3 % before). Category labels under the floor get 0.94 of their categories' share of its edge's width on the screen, wrap at their spaces when they do not fit and turn by 45° when a word alone does not (local/wrap3d: clustered columns 300 to 840 pt wide, names of one to three words, turned 20°, 300° and 330°: 96.3 %). Known deviations: the topmost contour of wireframe cells rising like a plane, which PowerPoint draws in the band above (not in saddles, whose contours match), and labels on the edge of fitting under an edge rising 32° (local/backview slide 6), which PowerPoint wraps where the box's size with single-line labels lets them fit.

<a id="chart.analysis"></a>

### Trendlines and error bars

`chart.analysis` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Regression fits (linear, exponential, logarithmic, polynomial, power, moving average) and error bars.

**Office behaviour:** Trendlines: linear, exponential, logarithmic, polynomial (order 2-6), power and moving average, with forward/backward forecast and fixed intercepts, drawn 1.5 pt dotted in the series color by default and clipped to the plot area. Equation and R squared labels show coefficients rounded to 4 decimals; the block is vertically centered on the trendline's end, its right edge 8.8 pt left of it (measured). Legend entries follow the series; a dashed key line (the dotted trendline's, or a series') gives every entry a longer key of 31.5 pt (a solid trendline does not). Error bars: fixed value, percentage, standard error, custom values and standard deviation (around the mean of all values, like Office), 4.5 pt caps; they extend the value axis. A trendline without a line of its own takes the Office 2007 style's: the theme's line style 1 in tx1 (LibreOffice's chart2/tdf127720, a 1 px black line in style 18, 88.2 → 95.0 %); PowerPoint writes its 1.5 pt dotted line into the files it saves.

<a id="chart.userShapes"></a>

### Shapes drawn on charts

`chart.userShapes` · P3 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Shapes anchored in relative or absolute chart coordinates; drawn with the normal shape code.

**Office behaviour:** A chart's drawing part (c:userShapes) anchors shapes by fractions of the chart frame (relSizeAnchor from/to) or by a corner and a size (absSizeAnchor); the shapes are DrawingML shapes, groups, pictures and connectors like on slides, their text without a size takes the chart's text properties, else 10 pt (measured with generated/charts-more).

`chart.pivot` · P3 · difficulty 1 · done

**Rendering:** Field buttons are not shown in presentations; pivot formats apply like data point formats.

<a id="chart.ex"></a>

### Office 2016 charts (chartex)

`chart.ex` · P2 · difficulty 4 · done

Waterfall, treemap, sunburst, histogram, Pareto, box and whisker, funnel and region map.

**Rendering:** Each type needs its own layout (treemap squarify, sunburst rings, histogram binning, box plot statistics); region maps use the geography data cached in the file. Check whether mc:Fallback contains a usable picture.

**Office behaviour:** PowerPoint saves Office 2016 charts as mc:AlternateContent (Requires cx1/cx2) with a picture of the chart in mc:Fallback, which PowerPoint does not show: it draws the chart itself, its default words in Office's language, where the picture has those of the Office that saved it (openxml-sdk's Of16-01 89 → 99 %, Of16-02 75 → 99 %; a German "Standard" format code is General). So the chart is laid out natively, in Word documents as in decks (docx chart.ex): a classic frame built from it (title, legend, axes) with layout rules measured on probe decks of 130 to 450 pt wide charts (local chartex, chartex-p1 … p4, chartex-pareto: 96–99 %): the plot area 6 pt from the edges and 4.25 pt below the title's line, axis labels 6.9 pt off it, category labels in a row, staggered up to 1.3 slots, turned by −30° up to 1.65 and −60° up to 2.15 slots, else upright; legends packed in rows of at most 80 % of the chart; histogram bins of Scott's width to two significant digits without axis headroom; waterfall totals and connectors; funnels; Pareto lines on a percentage axis; box plots (exclusive or inclusive quartiles, 1.5 IQR whiskers, outliers, means: the series share the category's width over 1 + gapWidth, a single one filling it, several each 1 / (1.105 n − 0.0525) of it and 1.105 boxes apart from its left edge less half their line, caps 0.3 of the box wide, the mean's cross 6 pt, outliers dots of 3 pt outlined, the category axis at the value axis's minimum and hidden outliers out of its range; measured in Word with docx/local/bxw-*: one to three series, gap widths 0 to 2, one to three categories, within 0.3 pt, 41–96 → 94–98 %; local/chartex and chartex-p1 box plot slides 95.9 → 98.8 %, 97.4 → 98.9 %); squarified treemaps with overlapping or banner labels; sunbursts sorted by size with radial, wrapped labels. Region maps (local chartex-map … map4 at 300 to 900 pt: 98.4–99.9 %) are drawn from their geography cache (raw deflated XML whose rings are Bing point compressed in the base64url alphabet) in Robinson's projection, 1.5 % wider than defined, fitted beside a color scale legend (interpolated in linear light) with the map's credits below; like PowerPoint's own pictures of such files, every region is gray, as the cache does not tell which region a data point names. A color style that varies one color (meth withinLinear, Office's monochromatic palettes) spreads its n colors evenly from a shade to a tint, the i-th v = 0.7 (2i + 1 − n) / (n + 1) of the way in linear light (docx/local/bxl-*: box plots of one to six series on accent1 and accent2, 29 colors within one level; a waterfall's three kinds take the first three of four, openxml-sdk/Of16-07). Axis titles (cx:axis cx:title) read Office's words where they have no text. Word lays these charts out with measures of its own (docx/local/wfl-*: waterfall legends of 7 to 16 pt at the top, bottom and right, axis titles of 10 and 14 pt, 98.5–99.4 %): the chart area as the style part has it (white, outlined in D9D9D9; PowerPoint draws none), its words in its own language (the engine's culture: "Diagrammtitel", "Anstieg", "Achsentitel"); legend keys of 7.2 pt whatever the text's size, 3.9 pt before their text, entries 7.4 pt apart, rows at least 14.08 pt high, the row at the top 9.2 pt below the title's line (10.96 pt inside the chart without one) and 1.62 pt right of the middle, the plot area 9.16 pt below it, 10.27 pt above a legend at the bottom and 13.68 pt from one at the side; axis titles at the edge with their axis's labels their lines' height and 6.6 pt further in; value labels half a line above the plot area also below a title alone. Not reproduced: the ellipsis PowerPoint puts on long category labels in very short charts.

<a id="smartart.drawing"></a>

### Saved SmartArt drawing

`smartart.drawing` · P1 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Draw the shapes from the diagram's drawing part like normal shapes.

**Office behaviour:** PowerPoint stores the laid-out shapes of every SmartArt in a drawing part (dsp:drawing), so files saved by PowerPoint need no SmartArt layout engine. The drawing is not complete, though: PowerPoint draws the text in the color the color definition gives the shape's style label (txFillClrLst, by the presentation point's presStyleIdx/presStyleCnt) where the drawing keeps the quick style's fontRef color (white text on the white boxes of the outline color schemes otherwise); it formats text by the layout's list style (Office 2016 extension dgm1612:lstStyle, by the depth of the text's data point, which the drawing loses: the layout tree tells which points each shape shows); and a 3D quick style's scene (styleDef/scene3d: camera with zoom and light rig) turns the whole diagram about its center, which the drawing does not keep either (Camera.Pivot, Camera.Zoom). A text rectangle (dsp:txXfrm) stands where the shape stands, turned by the shape's rotation plus its own (rot 90 with a text rot of -90 keeps the text of a turned chevron upright). Measured with all 159 layouts (98.8 %) and all 38 color definitions and 14 quick styles on a process (98.6 %, the rest 3D materials). A drawing part without shapes counts as missing: PowerPoint lays the diagram out anew (LibreOffice's SmartArt test decks, whose drawings are emptied).

<a id="smartart.data"></a>

### SmartArt data model

`smartart.data` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** The text and structure of the nodes; needed for editing and for files without a drawing part.

**Office behaviour:** PowerPoint keeps the laid-out shapes in the drawing part (smartart.drawing); the data model adds the diagram's own formatting: dgm:bg (fill and effects, e.g. a shadow) is drawn as a rectangle over the frame behind the shapes and dgm:whole (line and effects) on top of them (measured with generated/smartart: 99.2 %). The diagram is an element of its own sized like its frame, which animations of the whole diagram change; every shape carries the model id of its presentation point, which per-shape animations target (p:graphicEl/a:dgm with the drawing shape's model id; ids of data nodes or transitions animate nothing in PowerPoint, measured with its video export), and a:dgm bldStep=bg targets the background and outline. Checked against PowerPoint's video of generated/smartart slide 3 (background, a node fading, a node and its connector flying in, the rest appearing): 96.8 % (tools/Fidelity animate). The node structure (points, connections, presentation variables) only serves the layout engine for files without a drawing part (smartart.layout).
