<a id="smartart.layout"></a>

### SmartArt layout engine

`smartart.layout` · P3 · difficulty 5 · done

**Rendering:** Only for SmartArt without a saved drawing (files from other producers): layout algorithms (linear, snake, cycle, hierarchy, pyramid …) with constraints and rules.

**Office behaviour:** Only for SmartArt without a saved drawing (PowerPoint 2007 and other producers; PowerPoint itself always saves one). The layout tree is instantiated for the data model (all 159 built-in layouts match PowerPoint's presentation points; pos functions with an axis take the first selected point's position). Constraints: a node's own values first, those its ancestors give over them (the nearest last; equalizing and defaulting constraints do not count); a node showing a node's text counts as one for ptType; sp given to all descendants skips the lines laying out the owner's own point; a list's lte and gte bounds on other nodes hold where they stand and again until they hold together, a node's bounds on itself at the end, a value only bounds give follows them unless fitting fixed it, bounds on user variables hold nothing, and bounds between font sizes lower the larger one after fitting (an unset size being the default 36 pt), bounds with a negative factor hold nothing; a constraint without value or reference gives 0 only where nothing else gives a value, in the node's own list only where nothing before it does (HorizontalActionList's titles, h <= 0.3 w then h: 0.3 w high; arrow5's ring of eight, sibSp then sibSp <= 0.1 h: spaced 0); edges, centers and sizes are derived from each other with their offsets, references to a placed node read its laid-out box (sizes without its own offset), a root's child taller than the diagram as high as it (a square one keeping its shape), references to a node no instance of which exists take the value the list would give it, and every node evaluates its list again once its parent's algorithm gives it its box. Rules lower their targets toward val, or toward fact times what the target's value refers to; a lin node's font size rule also applies while a child is larger across the line than the node, strictly (LibreOffice's smartart-dir: hList1's columns at 31 pt in a frame 182.5 pt high, 56 -> 99.8 %), the children measured once the bounds their own lists set have settled (process3's descriptions below titles h <= 0.4 w: the row keeps its width and the titles their 34 pt; LibreOffice's smartart-accent-process 72.1 -> 98.6 %). Text whose runs all have sizes of their own keeps them and is not fitted; its margins follow that size, while boxes sized from its font size keep the size the constraints gave (LibreOffice's fill-color-list: titles of 18 pt, descriptions 1.22 x 65 high, 51 -> 100 %). Algorithms: composite (the room of all its children's boxes, invisible leaves included, a child taking its box keeping the bounds the composite sets between its width and height by giving up room across, centered, a lin child without a size taking its box; ar keeps its box while references see its constraint size; a composite root's drawing centered in the frame), lin (its rules on the children's length first, by halving to within a point of the room, font size rules in whole points, then uniform shrinking, a shrunk composite taking its content's room, along and, without a size of its own, across; a lin child whose children all stay narrower across its line taking their room; the row as long as the farthest a child reaches; sp only between neighbors without a spacer node; the row aligned in the node and each child across it; fallback 2D laid out at the own size and scaled; a tree alone on the line centered by itself; children's subtrees evaluated for its box before they are measured), snake (rules in 32nds of the way, fixed and balanced breakpoints, cells in place by their box and the grid aligned by the room their shapes take, short rows from their start unless off ctr, each row alignOff further, rows growing from grDir, cells never grown, connectors with a size spacing the cells, line gaps following the placed cells they refer to), cycle (the circle as small as keeps each two neighbors' outlines, an ellipse's or a box's as laid out, not turned along the path, sibSp apart along the line between them, each next but one at least touching, and the ring sp from the middle shape; measured with arrow5 and arrow1 at 3 to 8 nodes and sibSp from -0.9 to 0.3 times their width, 26 rings within 0.2 %: three nodes never closer than touching, seven spaced by the box their turned neighbors would not have; the node's rules in 32nds before shrinking; a ring with room to spare spreading out up to its own diameter, the shapes keeping their size; curved connectors as circular arrows or arcs over their span, padded along the arc), hierRoot/hierChild (subtrees as close as the shapes lying side by side allow, sibSp apart; rows lined up with the root's box and hung sp beyond its shapes; parents centered over their first and last child's head; assistants on a level of their own, in a horizontal chart a column of their own half their sibSp above the line; bent connectors; texts' values evaluated again as drawn), pyra (text rectangles 0.65 of a level's width, the apex's whole box), connectors between the shapes they join (named ends found inside the transitions' nodes, the diagram's own, the siblings named or the one node of that name; straight ones between named points, an automatic end at the middle of its node's side nearest to the named one, the radial points where a cycle's circle meets the nodes, or a center; placed again once laid out where they join nodes outside their parent; connDist made equal at the shortest; curved ones with a diameter along their circle between their named points, a diameter of connDist following it, negative ones counterclockwise, one from a node back to itself in a cycle closing the ring short of the node by 0.09°, a band without heads in a cycle a quarter of its width further in; stems alone 0.6 of the height without heads; lines without a height having none). Shapes turned by their rot take the size whose turned bounds are their box; a composite root is centered where its drawing fits the frame with its own connectors. Text fitting: every line its font's height times the line spacing, paragraphs apart by their space after, within the geometry's text rectangle and the margins (a boxless text node within the shape showing it); 36 pt where no constraint gives a size; font sizes in whole points, secondary sizes by their own rules, equ groups at their smallest; heights growing in sixteenths as far as the parent has room and lte bounds allow, widths growing in 32nds of the way to a rule's limit; descendants' text at the top level unless bullets start at its depth; the diagram laid out again with the pinned values. The output writes the shapes the way PowerPoint writes its drawing (text nodes' text taken by shapes showing the same data, styles and colors by label); a data point's own formatting (dgm:pt/dgm:spPr) colors the shape showing its text, unless that shape hides its geometry, before the shape's presentation point's (LibreOffice's smartart-cycle-matrix: 75.3 to 98.7 %); bulleted text hangs by 4.5 pt per size class (4.5 pt below 12 pt, 9 below 16, 13.5 below 20, 18 below 28, then 22.5; measured on PowerPoint's drawings of one diagram at 22 sizes). Measured against PowerPoint's drawings of the 159 layouts: 1349 of 1455 shapes within 1 %, 146 diagrams exact, 745 of 823 font sizes (151 shapes and 12 diagrams before this engine work); against generated decks of snake, picture, cycle, radial and hierarchy layouts at 1 to 8 nodes: 541 of 542, 550 of 594, 311 of 320, 412 of 427 and 578 of 690 shapes; rendered with the drawings removed 94.1 % (38.5 % before), all color definitions and quick styles on a process 97.9 %. Known deviations (only for diagrams saved without their drawing): HorizontalActionList's description boxes, which PowerPoint keeps at one height for two or more nodes that depends on the frame alone (250.3 pt in 840 x 460, 133 in 840 x 300, 285.1 in 600 x 460; one node fills the composite), AscendingPictureAccentProcess's drawing, which PowerPoint shrinks by 0.74 to 0.93 for 2 to 5 nodes and centers by its outline, BendingPictureBlocks' pictures overhanging their composites, cycle3's long curves, NameandTitleOrganizationalChart's names narrower than their constraint and radial2's ring (2 % smaller), each within 1 to 7 % (sa_all: 13 of 159 layouts not exact), and process3 with a description longer than its box, for which PowerPoint lowers the titles' font size the description's follows and grows the box, where the engine lets the description overflow. A lin node shrinks to its children along its line as well as across it, and its parent aligns it by nodeHorzAlign or nodeVertAlign: a row of vList5 whose node has no children is as wide as its parent text and stands at the left with nodeHorzAlign l, in the middle with ctr, at the right with r, whatever the row's own direction and horzAlign (local/vlist-*, layout variants laid out by PowerPoint; LibreOffice's sd/smartart-vertical-block-list 76.0 → 99.6 %, the probe decks sa_* unchanged).

**Elements (24):** [`dgm:adj`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmadj), [`dgm:adjLst`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmadjlst), [`dgm:alg`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmalg), [`dgm:cat`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmcat), [`dgm:catLst`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmcatlst), [`dgm:choose`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmchoose), [`dgm:clrData`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmclrdata), [`dgm:constr`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmconstr), [`dgm:constrLst`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmconstrlst), [`dgm:desc`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmdesc), [`dgm:else`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmelse), [`dgm:forEach`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmforeach), [`dgm:if`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmif), [`dgm:layoutDef`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmlayoutdef), [`dgm:layoutNode`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmlayoutnode), [`dgm:param`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmparam), [`dgm:presOf`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmpresof), [`dgm:rule`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmrule), [`dgm:ruleLst`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmrulelst), [`dgm:sampData`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmsampdata), [`dgm:shape`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmshape), [`dgm:styleData`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmstyledata), [`dgm:title`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmtitle), [`dgm:varLst`](../format/namespaces/dgm.md#dgmvarlst)

<a id="smartart.styles"></a>

### SmartArt styles and color definitions

`smartart.styles` · P3 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Part of the layout engine path.

**Office behaviour:** Color definitions: each style label lists fill, line, effect and text colors applied in turn (repeat), spread from the first to the last color (span: shape i of n at i/(n-1)) or there and back (cycle: 2i/n up to the middle, then back), spread colors written as the first with the HSL and alpha differences added (hueOff, satOff, lumOff, alphaOff; hue clockwise unless hueDir=ccw); PowerPoint's offsets match HSL differences computed with the sRGB transfer function for tint and shade. The index is the presentation point's presStyleIdx of presStyleCnt: shapes showing the same data points share one, decorations count each. Text color from txFillClrLst, else the quick style's fontRef. The quick style's scene3d turns the whole diagram (3d5 to 3d9 styles); its labels give style references, sp3d and light rigs, which the drawing keeps; its labels' text properties (dgm:txPr, the 3d9 style's a:sp3d extrusion) go into the text body's a:bodyPr. Applied to diagrams with a saved drawing (smartart.drawing) and to those the engine lays out (smartart.layout writes style references, color list colors, 3D properties and text properties per label like PowerPoint's drawings; all color definitions and quick styles on a process with the drawings removed: 95.7 %). Built-in color definitions leave txLinClrLst and txEffectClrLst empty; they are not applied.

<a id="transition.classic"></a>

### Classic transitions

`transition.classic` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Two stacked slide layers with SVG masks, transforms and opacity; WebGL for the 3D ones.

**Office behaviour:** Compared with PowerPoint's videos (tools/Fidelity transitions): 95.2 % over generated/transitions-classic. Masks with the soft edges PowerPoint draws for wipes, splits, strips, shapes and wheels; push, cover, uncover, comb, zoom and newsflash with PowerPoint's easing; blinds and checkerboard turn bands and tiles over in 3D as PowerPoint plays them since 2013; dissolve and random bars use random patterns of their own.

<a id="transition.office2010"></a>

### Office 2010 transitions

`transition.office2010` · P2 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** The slides (or their content and backgrounds apart) rasterized into textures and drawn in a WebGL scene with PowerPoint's camera; particle-like ones (vortex, shred, honeycomb, glitter) as many small tiles.

**Office behaviour:** All of them, compared with PowerPoint's videos: 83.4 % over generated/transitions-2010. Prism, cube, box, orbit, flip, switch, gallery, doors, window, ferris wheel, conveyor, pan, fly through, warp, reveal and flash as WebGL scenes whose poses and timing are fitted frame by frame; ripple as a growing soft circle; glitter, vortex, shred and honeycomb as turning tiles and particles with PowerPoint's timing, their random patterns differing from PowerPoint's. Written as mc:AlternateContent with a p:fade fallback.

**Elements (19):** [`p14:conveyor`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14conveyor), [`p14:doors`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14doors), [`p14:ferris`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14ferris), [`p14:flash`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14flash), [`p14:flip`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14flip), [`p14:flythrough`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14flythrough), [`p14:gallery`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14gallery), [`p14:glitter`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14glitter), [`p14:honeycomb`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14honeycomb), [`p14:pan`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14pan), [`p14:prism`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14prism), [`p14:reveal`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14reveal), [`p14:ripple`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14ripple), [`p14:shred`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14shred), [`p14:switch`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14switch), [`p14:vortex`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14vortex), [`p14:warp`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14warp), [`p14:wheelReverse`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14wheelreverse), [`p14:window`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14window)

<a id="animation.timing"></a>

### Timing tree

`animation.timing` · P1 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** A JS timing engine for the SMIL-like time containers (par, seq, excl): click sequences, with/after previous, triggers on shapes and media bookmarks, fill, repeat, auto-reverse, acceleration and deceleration.

**Office behaviour:** Checked against PowerPoint's own video of the slides (tools/Fidelity animate: slideshow 99.9 %, smartart 99.9 %, animations-charts 99.6 %, animations-text 100.0 %, animations-gallery 99.7 % of frames). Acceleration and deceleration follow a smoothstep speed curve (measured on six accel/decel pairs). A behaviour without fill of its own reverts when it ends, a set without duration holds; formulas take the current values (ppt_x, ppt_y …, not #ppt_x) as they are when their behaviour begins, a behaviour ending just then with its end (Bounce Out's fall and bounces build on each other). Text built letter by letter or word by word (p:iterate lt, wd; el, the default, animates the whole target): the shape's background first for the effect's duration when the build animates it (bldP animBg) and for emphases, then every letter or word (blanks are none) with the whole effect, tmPct of the effect's duration or tmAbs apart; a group after the previous effect starts when the groups before it end (the stored delay is the editor's guess, which iterated text outlasts). After effects and sounds (p:subTnLst): sameClick ones when their effect begins or ends as their condition says, nextClick ones (dim, hide on next click) when the next effect of the sequence begins. Containers repeat (repeatCount, repeatDur); indefinite repeats go on after their step until the end of the slide, or until the next click with an onNext end condition (checked live). A container ends with its last child (endSync all), with its first under endSync first; an exclusive container (p:excl) stops the child playing when another begins. Interactive sequences (triggers) play a click group per click on their shape or bookmark; once played (endSync all) the next click starts them again (restart whenNotActive), not with restart never. Effects with fill=remove rewind when done, tmFilter remaps progress, p14:bounceEnd swings about the end as a damped spring over its share of the duration (fitted on five videos). PowerPoint's video clock advances whole milliseconds per frame (33 ms at 30 fps) within a step while steps follow the video's frames; the player's frame mode does the same.

**Elements (19):** [`p:cTn`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pctn), [`p:childTnLst`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pchildtnlst), [`p:cond`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pcond), [`p:endCondLst`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pendcondlst), [`p:endSync`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pendsync), [`p:excl`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pexcl), [`p:iterate`](../format/namespaces/p.md#piterate), [`p:nextCondLst`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pnextcondlst), [`p:par`](../format/namespaces/p.md#ppar), [`p:prevCondLst`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pprevcondlst), [`p:rtn`](../format/namespaces/p.md#prtn), [`p:seq`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pseq), [`p:stCondLst`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pstcondlst), [`p:subTnLst`](../format/namespaces/p.md#psubtnlst), [`p:timing`](../format/namespaces/p.md#ptiming), [`p:tmAbs`](../format/namespaces/p.md#ptmabs), [`p:tmPct`](../format/namespaces/p.md#ptmpct), [`p:tn`](../format/namespaces/p.md#ptn), [`p:tnLst`](../format/namespaces/p.md#ptnlst)

**Specification:** §19.3.1.48 Slide Timing Information for a Slide Layout (p.2601); §19.5.25 Children Time Node List (p.2640); §19.5.31 Condition (p.2645); §19.5.33 Common Time Node Properties (p.2648); §19.5.37 End Conditions List (p.2652); §19.5.39 EndSync (p.2653) and 13 more

<a id="animation.behaviors"></a>

### Animation behaviours

`animation.behaviors` · P1 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Attribute names (ppt_x, ppt_y, ppt_w, ppt_h, style.rotation, style.opacity, style.visibility, fillcolor …) map to properties of the rendered elements; animEffect filters (fade, wipe, barn, blinds, circle …) become SVG masks; motion paths are relative path strings.

**Office behaviour:** Formulas use ppt_x, ppt_y, ppt_w, ppt_h (the property's current value as the behaviour begins, used by exit effects such as Fly Out and Bounce Out) besides #ppt_x … (the value before the animation). Motion paths move at a speed even in the path's own units (fractions of the slide's width and height), not on the screen (measured with a circle path: 1.1 px against 7.6 px). Filters (measured frame by frame with PowerPoint's videos of every filter and direction, entrance and exit): every point of the shape's drawn bounds (its outline included) has a moment u at which the edge passes it, in the bounds' own proportions: the distance from the starting side (wipe; blinds in 6 bands; checkerboard in 6 x 6 cells, odd rows half a cell over), from the middle (box, circle reaching the corners, diamond, plus, barn), the angle from 12 o'clock (wedge both ways, wheel per spoke), the diagonal of a 16 x 16 block grid (strips, u = d/31). The edge is a linear ramp 0.14 wide from just before u = 0 to just past u = 1. Exits hide what the edge passes; blinds, checkerboard and strips play their entrance backwards instead. Dissolve shows one-pixel specks at the pace of the average of three random numbers (an S curve), random bars one-pixel lines at a measured pace. The player draws all of them as SVG masks (gradients, fans, a thresholded noise image) on the shape's drawing, so outlines past the shape's box stay; groups get a CSS mask over their box. Color animations work in RGB and HSL (to, by; the hue turns the way dir says whatever the sign of by, Complementary Color 2). Text formatting (style.fontWeight, style.fontStyle, style.textDecorationUnderline, style.fontFamily) shows the text laid out anew in the real faces, as PowerPoint does: the converter draws it in every formatting the timing gives it, per paragraph or, for text built letter by letter, per letter (lined up with the letter it stands for); style.fontSize scales the text about its anchor and ends in the text laid out at that size. fill.on gives a shape without fill the animated fill; stroke.on, fill.opacity, stroke.opacity, stroke.weight, ppt_r and imageData.grayscale (VML-era names PowerPoint's presets do not use) are applied too. Effects of an iterated build animate the background first, the font size scales only the text (generated/animations-gallery: 99.7 % of 373 video frames over 12 slides of PowerPoint's effect gallery).

**Elements (26):** [`p:anim`](../format/namespaces/p.md#panim), [`p:animClr`](../format/namespaces/p.md#panimclr), [`p:animEffect`](../format/namespaces/p.md#panimeffect), [`p:animMotion`](../format/namespaces/p.md#panimmotion), [`p:animRot`](../format/namespaces/p.md#panimrot), [`p:animScale`](../format/namespaces/p.md#panimscale), [`p:attrName`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pattrname), [`p:attrNameLst`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pattrnamelst), [`p:boolVal`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pboolval), [`p:by`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pby), [`p:cBhvr`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pcbhvr), [`p:clrVal`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pclrval), [`p:cmd`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pcmd), [`p:fltVal`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pfltval), [`p:from`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pfrom), [`p:hsl`](../format/namespaces/p.md#phsl), [`p:intVal`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pintval), [`p:progress`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pprogress), [`p:rCtr`](../format/namespaces/p.md#prctr), [`p:rgb`](../format/namespaces/p.md#prgb), [`p:set`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pset), [`p:strVal`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pstrval), [`p:tav`](../format/namespaces/p.md#ptav), [`p:tavLst`](../format/namespaces/p.md#ptavlst), [`p:to`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pto), [`p:val`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pval)

<a id="animation.build"></a>

### Build lists

`animation.build` · P1 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Build text by paragraph and level, charts by series or category, SmartArt by node.

**Office behaviour:** SmartArt builds (a:bldDgm one, lvlOne, lvlAtOnce) are written as effects per drawing shape (p:graphicEl with a:dgm id = the shape's model id, bldStep bg for the diagram's background); the player animates each shape of the diagram on its own (checked with tools/Fidelity animate on generated/smartart). Chart builds (a:bldChart series, category, seriesEl, categoryEl) are effects per a:chart step: gridLegend (everything but the data), series, category, ptInSeries, ptInCategory, with seriesIdx -3/-4 and categoryIdx -3/-4 where they do not apply (PowerPoint keeps them so; its object model refuses chart builds of modern charts). PowerPoint animates a series or category as one: a wipe runs over the box around all its bars, labels and markers; a line segment builds with the category it ends at. The chart marks its parts (data-ser in plot order, data-pt), the player animates their union (generated/animations-charts: 99.5 % of 103 video frames). Builds of legacy OLE charts (p:bldOleChart) animate the whole object, which is drawn from its picture.

**Elements (11):** [`a:bldChart`](../format/namespaces/a.md#abldchart), [`a:bldDgm`](../format/namespaces/a.md#ablddgm), [`p:bldAsOne`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pbldasone), [`p:bldDgm`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pblddgm), [`p:bldGraphic`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pbldgraphic), [`p:bldLst`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pbldlst), [`p:bldOleChart`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pbldolechart), [`p:bldP`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pbldp), [`p:bldSub`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pbldsub), [`p:tmpl`](../format/namespaces/p.md#ptmpl), [`p:tmplLst`](../format/namespaces/p.md#ptmpllst)

<a id="animation.media"></a>

### Media control and bookmarks

`animation.media` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Play, pause and stop commands and media bookmarks as timing events.

**Office behaviour:** Media nodes (p:audio, p:video, cMediaNode) give volume and mute, showWhenStopped 0 (the shape hides in the show while the medium is not playing), numSld (it plays on across that many slides), fullScrn (a video fills the slide, centered, while it plays) and isNarration (narration media stay silent when the show plays without narrations); p:cmd plays, pauses, resumes, stops and seeks them; bookmarks (p14:bmkTgt) start trigger sequences when playback reaches them; sounds of effects (p:sndTgt in the effect's p:subTnLst, begin of the effect) play with the effect. What was done during a recorded show (p14:showEvtLst in the slide's extLst: play, pause, resume, stop, seek of media, clicks on triggers) replays at its times from the start of the slide's timeline when the show uses its timings. Checked live in the browser with probe decks (times within 10 ms).

**Elements (12):** [`p14:bmkTgt`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14bmktgt), [`p14:nullEvt`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14nullevt), [`p14:pauseEvt`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14pauseevt), [`p14:playEvt`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14playevt), [`p14:resumeEvt`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14resumeevt), [`p14:seekEvt`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14seekevt), [`p14:showEvtLst`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14showevtlst), [`p14:stopEvt`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14stopevt), [`p14:triggerEvt`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14triggerevt), [`p:audio`](../format/namespaces/p.md#paudio), [`p:cMediaNode`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pcmedianode), [`p:video`](../format/namespaces/p.md#pvideo)

<a id="animation.pictures"></a>

### Animated pictures

`animation.pictures` · P3 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Play the embedded animations of 3D models in the slide show with our own glTF renderer: skins, node transforms (linear, step and cubic spline keys) and morph targets, posed at the effect's progress.

**Office behaviour:** A 3D model's embedded animations (a3danim:embedAnim with aanim:animPr, a3danim:posterFrame) play in the slide show only through the slide's timing: PowerPoint adds a Scene effect (presetID 100, emph, with the slide's start, repeatCount indefinite) whose p:anim takes attrName embeddedN from 0 to 1: the glTF file's animation N - 1 (not embedAnim's animId) over its whole length, stretched to the effect's duration; without the effect the model stands still at its poster frame (measured with PowerPoint's videos). animPr (length, count, auto, offset, st, end) and the poster frame describe the playback in PowerPoint's own views; the document shows the poster frame, as printed. PowerPoint offers only skeletal animations: a model whose animations move plain nodes or morph targets gets none. Before its first key a cubic spline is, in PowerPoint, that key's in-tangent. Checked with generated/models (99.8 % of PowerPoint's video frames) and a rigged fox (local, 99.7 %).

**Elements (1):** [`aanim:animPr`](../format/namespaces/aanim.md#aanimanimpr)

**Specification:** MS-ODRAWXML §2.37.1.1 animPr (p.316)

<a id="animation.laser"></a>

### Recorded laser pointer

`animation.laser` · P3 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Replay recorded laser pointer movements with the narration.

**Office behaviour:** A recorded laser pointer (p14:laserTraceLst in the slide's extLst) replays in the slide show when narrations play (showPr showNarration; without showPr PowerPoint plays them, measured): the pointer, in p14:laserClr of the show settings (red by default), follows every trace (p14:tracePtLst) from its first to its last point, at the points' times (ST_UniversalTimeOffset, milliseconds without a unit) from the start of the slide's timeline after the transition, moving straight between the recorded points (x, y in EMU from the slide's top left corner). PowerPoint draws nothing of it on the slide itself. Checked with generated/laser (slide unchanged; replay positions checked in the browser).

**Elements (4):** [`p14:laserClr`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14laserclr), [`p14:laserTraceLst`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14lasertracelst), [`p14:tracePt`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14tracept), [`p14:tracePtLst`](../format/namespaces/p14.md#p14traceptlst)

**Specification:** MS-PPTX §2.3.1.16 laserClr (p.33); MS-PPTX §2.3.1.17 laserTraceLst (p.33); MS-PPTX §2.3.3.7 CT_LaserTrace (p.44); MS-PPTX §2.3.3.9 CT_LaserTracePoint (p.45)

### Slide show player

<a id="interactivity.hyperlinks"></a>

### Hyperlinks and action settings

`interactivity.hyperlinks` · P1 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Links for URLs and files; ppaction:// actions (jump to slide, next, previous, first, last, end show, custom show) handled by the player; hover actions and sounds.

**Office behaviour:** Hyperlinked text is drawn in the theme's hyperlink color unless ahyp:hlinkClr says to keep the text color. Mouse-over actions (a:hlinkHover on shapes) run when the pointer enters the shape during the show. Mouse-over actions on text (a:hlinkMouseOver) run when the pointer enters the text during the show, like a shape's a:hlinkHover. highlightClick, invalidUrl and tgtFrame do not change the picture. Programs, macros and OLE verbs are deliberately not run from a web page.

<a id="interactivity.zoom"></a>

### Summary, section and slide zoom

`interactivity.zoom` · P2 · difficulty 3 · done

**Rendering:** Draw the zoom's picture and link it to the target slide or section; the player zooms in and back out.

**Office behaviour:** Zoom objects (slide, section and summary zooms) are drawn from their mc:Fallback pictures, linked to their slides (local/zoom: 99.6 %). In the slide show a click zooms into the target: the view closes in on the zoom's frame until the slide, growing out of it, fills the screen, over transitionDur (an even zoom in scale with a sine ease; without transitionDur the slide's own transition plays). With returnToParent (the default) the show zooms back out to the zoom's slide after the target slide or the last slide of the section. The zoom's motion is not measured against PowerPoint: its video export follows the slide order and a live show would need a real click.

<a id="review.comments"></a>

### Comments (classic)

`review.comments` · P3 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Optional comment markers and a side panel.

**Office behaviour:** Classic comments (p:cmLst, authors from commentAuthors.xml with their color index, replies joined to their thread through p15:threadingInfo/p15:parentCm) are never drawn by PowerPoint, neither on slides nor in the slide show. The document lists the threads below each slide on request (the player's comment button or C) with markers labelled with the author's initials and index at p:pos, which counts 1/576 inch from the slide's top left corner (PowerPoint's master units). Times are shown as stored. Checked with generated/comments.

### Modern comments, reactions and tasks

`review.modernComments` · P3 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Optional threaded comments in a side panel.

**Office behaviour:** Modern comments (p188:cmLst, authors from authors.xml, linked from the slide by p188:commentRel) are listed with their replies, status (resolved, closed), reactions (p223: the emoji with the reacting authors) and tasks (assignees, start and due date, progress and title from the comment's attributes, or replayed from the p228 history without undone events). Markers sit at p188:pos in EMU from the top left corner of the anchor: the slide, or the shape that ac:deMkLst or ac:txMkLst names by id or creationId. Created times are UTC and shown in the viewer's time zone. Checked with generated/comments; PowerPoint draws none of it on slides.

<a id="legacy.vml"></a>

### VML drawings

`legacy.vml` · P3 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Only for very old converted files and controls: prefer the DrawingML fallback (the picture of an OLE object or control); a VML renderer is a separate project.

**Office behaviour:** PowerPoint 2007 saved the pictures of OLE objects and ActiveX controls only in a VML drawing part of the slide: p:oleObj and p:control name their VML shape (spid), whose v:imagedata (o:relid or r:id, croptop/bottom/left/right as fractions or 1/65536ths) holds the picture; later versions add a DrawingML picture in p:pic and write the VML choice without any picture (mc:Choice Requires=v, so the fallback is taken). Without a DrawingML picture the document draws the VML one in the object's frame, a control at the place its VML shape's style gives (PowerPoint does the same: measured with a VML picture differing from the object, generated/legacy-vml 99.8 %). HTML-like VML (unclosed br) is closed before parsing. Slides hold no other VML content.

<a id="engine"></a>

## Rendering engine (cross-cutting)

Features without elements of their own that decide the fidelity of everything else.

| Feature | Priority | Difficulty | Status | Elements | Office notes |
|---|---|---|---|---:|---:|
| [HTML output](#engine.output) | P0 | ●●○○○ | done | 0 | 0 |
| [Text measurement and line breaking](#engine.textLayout) | P0 | ●●●●● | done | 0 | 0 |
| [Font resolution and fallback](#engine.fonts) | P0 | ●●●●○ | done | 0 | 0 |
| [Fidelity measurement](#engine.fidelity) | P0 | ●●○○○ | done | 0 | 0 |

<a id="engine.output"></a>

### HTML output

`engine.output` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** One self-contained HTML document (or folder) per presentation: slides as fixed-size containers at 96 dpi, scaled to the viewport; images and fonts embedded or next to it; no external dependencies. Slides can be drawn for device pixels of their own (PixelGrid): scaled to them, outlines, rectangles and chart lines placed on the slide's pixels first and then where PowerPoint's antialiasing samples the device's, text and table borders on the device's.

**Office behaviour:** Every corpus deck converts to one self-contained document. PowerPoint's own picture of a slide whose size is no whole number of pixels covers the size rounded half up on each side, shapes across as on the slide and down scaled by (h / H)(W / w), text and tables by w / W across and h / H down (measured with probe decks of one-pixel bars at 1058.33 × 793.67, 907.17 × 604.67, 1280.5 × 720, 1209.5 × 604.67 and 1350 × 759.33 px, of one-cell tables and of text boxes); outlines keep the places they take on the slide's pixels and are antialiased where they land (a 1 px outline at 60.3 px of a 793.67 px slide drawn 794 high covers 3/4 of row 60 and 1/4 of row 61). Asked for another size, PowerPoint resamples the picture in bands.

<a id="engine.textLayout"></a>

### Text measurement and line breaking

`engine.textLayout` · P0 · difficulty 5 · done

**Rendering:** Shape text with HarfBuzz using the actual font files, break lines like PowerPoint (UAX 14 plus Office's rules), justify and position every line ourselves; the browser only draws the positioned runs.

**Office behaviour:** Measured with the text-snap calibration deck: glyph advances, kerning and letter spacing are each rounded to master units (1/8 pt, half up); kerning uses the legacy 'kern' table (GDI pairs). Fonts without one are kerned from GPOS only when they are PostScript (CFF) or variable fonts (measured: Bahnschrift, Segoe UI Variable, Sitka and embedded Gotham and Wix Madefor Display are kerned; Gabriola, Javanese Text and Yu Mincho, static TrueType with GPOS pairs, are not). Within a format run the glyph origins are rounded to whole pixels (half up) from the run's exact start; a new format run starts exactly. Baselines sit on whole pixels counted from the top of the text area (box top plus inset, anchor offset included). Glyphs are drawn unhinted with 8×4 samples per pixel. From 30 pixels per em text is therefore drawn as glyph outlines (paths defined once per document, placed with use) at the exact positions, with the text transparent on top for selection and search; browser text would sit on whole-pixel baselines and be hinted. PowerPoint samples coverage at the top-left of 8 × 4 sub-pixels, Chromium at the centers of 4 sub-rows: outlines are shifted by 1/16 and 1/8 pixel to see the same edges. Verified with generated/text- metrics, text-snap and text-basic.

<a id="engine.fonts"></a>

### Font resolution and fallback

`engine.fonts` · P0 · difficulty 4 · done

**Rendering:** Find installed and embedded fonts, metric-compatible substitutes (Carlito for Calibri, Liberation for Arial and Times New Roman) and Office cloud fonts (Aptos); subset and embed them as WOFF2, so the browser draws with exactly the font we measured.

**Office behaviour:** PowerPoint renders text from 30 pixels per em on unhinted (cap height and x-height exactly as designed), below that hinted with crisp horizontal edges on whole-pixel baselines (22 pt hinted, 23 pt smooth at 96 dpi). Large text is emitted as glyph outlines, so the viewer needs no fonts for it; small text, color fonts, synthesized bold or italic and glyphs missing from the font are left to the browser, whose hinted rendering matches PowerPoint's small text closely (Arial exactly); fonts the browser cannot find by name are embedded for these runs. Installed fonts without a typographic family whose family name holds a weight or width are named by their faces' full names (local()): Windows groups them by the rest of the name, so Chrome drew "Franklin Gothic Medium" in Franklin Gothic Demi and found "Franklin Gothic Demi", "Eras Medium ITC" or "OCR A Extended" not at all (Times New Roman); all 30 such families on this machine are their own faces so. Verified with generated/text-basic, text-eastasian, text-more and probe/font.

<a id="engine.fidelity"></a>

### Fidelity measurement

`engine.fidelity` · P0 · difficulty 2 · done

**Rendering:** Reference renderings from PowerPoint (COM, Windows only) against screenshots of our HTML (headless Chromium), compared slide by slide; the scores show where to work next.

**Office behaviour:** tools/Fidelity generates the corpus, renders references with PowerPoint and scores every deck.

None: every element that can occur in a PPTX file belongs to at least one feature.
